# War-room ledger

Source of truth for the war-room backlog across loop iterations. The chair
updates it after every Round D ruling and every verified fix. One row per item;
never delete a row — move it to Closed or Declined with the evidence.

Status: `open` · `in-progress (<branch>)` · `fixed (<branch/PR>)` ·
`verified (<PR>, CI run)` · `blocked (<what is needed>)`.
Evidence tier as in `repo-map.md`.

| ID | Item | Area | Evidence | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A-1 | Payout wallet calls clamped to 10 s (`payments_rpc.go:37,64-74`) under the 30 s send bound; ambiguous send recorded failed, admin requeue can double-pay | payments | code read, verified | verified (PR #6; opsec-qa-release; fix/payout-timeout 12b3e71) |
| A-2 | No working restore/rollback for the default internal-db deployment; `UPGRADING.md` rollback impossible with `build: .` | ops | code read, verified | verified (PR #6; opsec-qa-release; fix/ops-restore-setup-token 868d94f) |
| A-23 | Monero payout send classifies every JSON-RPC error as "nothing broadcast" (`walletRejected`, `payments_watcher.go:547`); monero-wallet-rpc v0.18.5.1 returns -38 (no daemon connection) when `sendrawtransaction` times out after the daemon may have relayed, and -4 from post-submit checks → requeue skips the wallet confirmation → possible double pay (DA-1) | payments | source trace (DA), code read (chair) | verified (PR #7 @f66ab6b, CI 35908986249; opsec-qa-release, v0.18.5.1 source re-checked; fix/a23-monero-ambiguous-send 866210a, migration 055) |
| A-52 | `enqueuePayout` sums settled deposits and later marks `credited=true WHERE settled` in separate statements under READ COMMITTED while `recordIncoming` upserts without the order lock: a deposit reaching the threshold in between is credited but not paid and never flagged, and a counted deposit turning conflicted in between is paid out uncredited and never held — verifier reproduced 57/400 and 80/400 without any trigger (33 payouts included a deposit the ledger already marked conflicted) (HUNT-E-2, bug hunt #1) | payments | Go DB race, verified | verified (PR #7 @cdb69f2, CI 35913448670; opsec-qa-release with mutation checks; -race count=5 15/15, real-chain payout sum = credited sum (QA-reported; log not retained — DA9-8); fix/a52-enqueue-credit-race c40769a) |
| A-66 | After the documented side-by-side restore or rollback (`DATABASE_URL` switched to `opsecmkt_restored`/`opsecmkt_rollback`), `scripts/backup.sh` still dumps hard-coded `-d opsecmkt`: every later backup silently omits all live data; no documented path backs up the live internal database (HUNT2-D-1; repro: internal-db Compose transcript, verified) | ops | ops tier (real Compose + age), verified | verified (PR #8 @766c87f, CI 35952354841; opsec-qa-release with mutation checks; restore scripts under bash 5 with real Compose + age, no credentials in logs; fix/a66-backup-live-db e70c428) |
| A-150 | `/challenge` holds its transaction connection and a `FOR UPDATE` lock on the pending login, then calls `factor.Enrolled(ctx, a.db, …)` needing a second pool connection (pool cap 12): 13+ concurrent POSTs on one pending cookie exhaust the pool and freeze every page, admin action and watcher DB call until the 12 s request timeout (measured 12.1 s; a bystander GET 11.97 s); any registered user with a factor can repeat it per sign-in (BE11-1; chair confirmed the code path) | identity/ops | Go DB probe (backend seat), code read (chair) | verified (PR #9 @b80ab8f, CI 36034975984; opsec-qa-release: freeze reproduced on faea98b (burst 12.02 s, bystander 12.00 s), gone on b80ab8f 10/10; both tests fail when `c.Tx`→`a.db` or the 400/500 split is reverted; connector-level audit of pool use under an open request transaction over the full suite: 0 violations (2 on the revert); 30/30 under load, worst 427 ms) |
| A-97 | Unsent payouts on closed orders are sent on a stale ledger: `pollCurrency`'s watch set (`payments_watcher.go:158-163`) drops a terminal order 24 h after its last update once its credited deposits reach the threshold, and `sendPayouts` (:508-511) claims its pending payout without a fresh read — a blocked/failed/restore-held payout released later is sent although its deposit was reversed (never flagged or held); also `sendPayouts` runs for orders whose `settleOrder` failed this pass, leaving the unpinned claim re-check (:510) as the only guard (PAY9-1, reproduced by payments and independently by QA; DA9 MISSED; converse: a watcher-held payout on an address >30 days old never un-holds) | payments | Go DB + fake provider, reproduced twice | verified (PR #9 @483d944, CI 36006118748 attempt 2 — attempt 1 red only on the A-128 race; opsec-qa-release: pre-fix repro on 25432f4 fails / passes after, 21 mutations (20 killed; M15 has no observable effect), interplay with restore/suspension holds, Playwright 390 + wallet 4/4; fix/a97-send-after-fresh-read ffbc475) |

Chair rulings on deviations, all accepted: (1) when both parties signed and the two do not finalize together, the market co-signs with whichever party signature verifies and discards the other (A-339), reverting only when neither does — the outputs are frozen, so one party plus the market is the ordinary 2-of-3; (2) a signature is judged bad only when finalizepsbt is incomplete with the market's signature present or testmempoolaccept refuses a script; other refusals wait as mempool-rejected, and a short arbiter key range waits as market-key-missing; (3) A-342's bound is a fixed 24 hours from the request, keeping the 50 largest confirmed outputs and flagging the rest escrow_left_out for the stray refund; (4) a dispute decision may change until its settlement has a stored final transaction (the market signature exists only inside the co-sign step that stores it, so this is the ruling's "fixed once a market signature exists"); the replaced settlement is superseded and its outputs freed; (5) either party may sign a stray refund (its only output is the buyer's pinned refund address), one open per order, first daily check 24 h after escrow first runs; (6) an arbiter wallet that is not dedicated disables escrow rather than stopping startup, checked as descriptor wallet, private keys enabled, not encrypted, txcount 0 (listdescriptors stays off the whitelist; the operator guide says what is not checked); (7) keypoolrefill to market index + 1000 just before co-signing, when getaddressinfo says the key is not yet the wallet's. Existing tests changed only for ruled behaviour (A-340 second signer, disputes no longer 409, one open stray refund, 64-hex fixture txids, the A-341 pin now allowing the late output's own stray refund and additionally asserting the refund spends only its own output). ### Iteration 3 rulings (chair) — acceptance criteria

- **A-6 (ACCEPT)**: every open dispute visible to the viewer is always listed, open first, then recent resolved up to a cap with a truncation notice; vendor NeedsAction equals the true count of the vendor's `paid` orders and those orders are never hidden by the 100-row cap; no authorization change. Test: DB integration with one old open dispute + >100 newer resolved; vendor with >100 newer orders + an old paid order.
- **A-7 (MODIFY)**: keep one payout per order and today's payout sum. Watch every funded non-terminal order regardless of `updated` age; a deposit first recorded after funding writes one order event and notifies buyer and vendor (idempotent); funding-deposit conflict on an order older than 24 h is detected; implementation-status updated. Dissent recorded: re-routing post-funding extras to the buyer was rejected because it needs a second payout per order (breaks invariant 4). Test: DB integration via newPayEnv, orders with `updated` 48 h ago.
- **A-21 (ACCEPT)**: releasing a payout held by a restore (`error` prefixed `Restored from backup:`) requires `not_broadcast=confirmed`, like the ambiguous requeue; note and audit record the confirmation; watcher-held release unchanged; admin UI shows the checkbox only for restored-held payouts; docs updated. Test: DB integration mirroring the ambiguous-requeue tests.

**Round D (iteration 3 council). No majority vote; dissent kept.**

- **A-23 (MODIFY → P0)**. WHY: DA traced monero-wallet-rpc v0.18.5.1 (`wallet2::commit_tx` → `sendrawtransaction` timeout → `no_connection_to_daemon` → -38; post-submit checks → -4); chair confirmed `walletRejected` treats any `*rpcError` as definite. DISSENT: QA (Bitcoin Core commits the tx before relay, so a BTC JSON-RPC error means nothing was committed; an allowlist would train reflexive box-ticking) — accepted for BTC, so the change is Monero-only; DA wanted BTC allowlisted too. ACCEPTANCE: a Monero `transfer` error is definite ("nothing broadcast") only for codes raised before submission, verified against the v0.18.5.1 source and listed in one Go table with a comment citing it (expected: -2 wrong address, -16 tx not possible, -17 not enough money, -18 tx too large, -19 not enough outs, -20 zero destination, -37 not enough unlocked money — confirm each); every other code (incl. -38, -4, -1) stores `send_ambiguous=true` and requeue then needs `not_broadcast=confirmed`; BTC classification unchanged but documented with its reason; admin copy says "the wallet reported a pre-broadcast error". TEST: httptest JSON-RPC table cases in `payments_payout_timeout_test.go` for XMR allowlisted vs -38/-4/-1 and BTC unchanged; extend `TestAmbiguousFailedPayoutRequeueNeedsBroadcastConfirmation` with XMR -38. EXPERIMENT (A-45) settles the live behaviour.
- **A-24 (ACCEPT, minimal shape; disposition action deferred to A-31)**. USER IMPACT: moderators/admins can finally see what they were told to review. ACCEPTANCE: a non-party moderator or admin gets 200 read-only on `/order` for any order with a flagged payment (`payments.flagged`), with order history, deposit txids and flag notes visible, no buyer/vendor forms, and no digital delivery content unless the order is disputed; an unflagged, undisputed order still 404s for staff; /moderator gets a "Payment review" list driven by `payments.flagged` (order link, full order ID, currency, amount, full txid, flag time), newest first, capped with a truncation notice; runbook section on handling flags, including recording a manual refund as a written note (no payout row: `payouts.order_id` is unique — DA MISSED). TEST: Go DB `TestFlaggedOrderReadableByNotifiedStaff` (newPayEnv, conflict after paid; moderator+admin 200, party moderator unchanged, unflagged 404), desk listing assertion; update `gaps_integration_test.go` 404 pins only for flagged orders.
- **A-25 (ACCEPT)**. ACCEPTANCE: every payout needing attention (blocked, held, failed, stuck sending) is always listed with its resolve form, oldest first, then recent settled payouts up to 50 with a truncation notice; "N payouts need attention" shown; order IDs in the payout table link to the order (admin read access follows A-24 rules — link only where the admin can open it). TEST: Go DB `TestAdminPayoutsAttentionNeverHidden` (one failed + one stuck, then 60 newer sent).
- **A-26 (ACCEPT; merges DA-3 and MOD-5 role part; iteration 5: also A-71)**. ACCEPTANCE: the second-factor reset form also takes a handle looked up server-side (no 1000-row picker);  the role form takes a handle (text input, handle pattern) looked up server-side; with >100 newer users the oldest vendor can be demoted; unknown handle → 404 with the form re-rendered and input kept; admin role assignment still refused; A-4 audit rows unchanged. TEST: Go DB (vendor + 101 buyers); `db-admin.spec.ts` updated with fresh accounts.
- **A-27 (MODIFY)**: one admin action "Suspend / restore account". DISSENT: DA (suspension state is over-engineered; demotion + end-sessions suffice) — rejected because ending sessions without blocking sign-in does not contain a known password, and the check points are only sign-in and session load (sessions and pending logins are deleted). ACCEPTANCE: password(+TOTP)-confirmed, admin-only, refused for administrators; suspend ends all sessions and pending sign-ins and blocks new sign-in with a specific message; restore re-enables sign-in; both accounts audited naming the other; user notified; open orders continue for the counterparty; listings unchanged (demotion already archives). New numbered migration. TEST: Go DB `TestAdminSuspendsAccount`; `db-admin.spec.ts` with fresh accounts.
- **A-28 (MODIFY — host-only tool, no web path)**. ACCEPTANCE: `opsecmkt -reset-admin-password <handle>` (or equivalent flag on the server binary) reads the new password from stdin, applies the registration rule, hashes at the app's bcrypt cost, ends that admin's sessions and pending sign-ins and writes an audit row; refuses non-admin handles; operator-guide gives the exact `docker compose run` command and no hand-written SQL. TEST: Go DB test of the CLI path; `bash -n`; ops doc validated.
- **A-29 (MODIFY — runbook + tested SQL, no web action)**. DISSENT: PM originally proposed a web "record external payout"; withdrawn under MOD's hijack argument. ACCEPTANCE: `testnet-runbook.md` reconcile section becomes one ordered procedure (isolate the instance, reconcile, stop, clear gate, restart) with no contradiction; it provides the SQL to record a payout sent after the backup (sent payout row + order event + audit, inside one transaction, with a check query) and the order-state reconstruction; `scripts/test-backup-restore.sh` gains a case where a restored order with no payout row is reconstructed with that SQL and completing it queues no second payout. TEST: that script case in CI's operations job.
- **A-30 (ACCEPT; required before the next tag)**. ACCEPTANCE: startup refuses (non-zero exit, error names the version, nothing migrated or served) when `schema_migrations` holds a version the binary does not embed; normal start and the alpha.1 upgrade unchanged; `UPGRADING.md` rollback text matches. TEST: Go DB `TestStartupRefusesUnknownAppliedMigration`; `scripts/test-upgrade.sh` rollback rehearsal step.
- **A-52 (P0; raised from the verifier's REQUIRED by the chair: a payout including a deposit the ledger already marks conflicted breaks the hold invariant).** ACCEPTANCE (verifier's safe shape): `enqueuePayout` returns early when a payout row exists; otherwise `SELECT id,amount FROM payments WHERE <settled> FOR UPDATE`, sums in Go, inserts the payout, then `UPDATE payments SET credited=true WHERE id=ANY($ids)` — the payout amount equals exactly the credited rows; the hold check sees every credited row. NOTE: the implementer flagged the same SUM-then-UPDATE shape in `settleOrder`'s awaiting_payment→paid step; the chair ruled it unreachable — only the single advisory-locked watcher writes `payments`, and it records all deposits before settling any order in a pass, so nothing interleaves. TEST: the hunt's `TestHuntRefundSilentlyDropsDepositConfirmedDuringEnqueue` and the verifier's `TestVerifyE2NaturalRace`/`TestVerifyE2ReverseRace` (artifacts/bug-hunt/699ea45/verify/verify2_payments_test.go.txt) adapted into `payments_watcher_gaps_test.go`, passing repeatedly under `-race`.
- **A-53 (ACCEPT; extends the A-21 ruling)**: restore SQL (restore.sh, UPGRADING.md) also marks `failed` rows ambiguous and prefixes `Restored from backup:` (guarded for dumps without the column); restore output counts them; the admin label no longer says "nothing broadcast" for them; `test-backup-restore.sh` and `test-internal-db-restore.sh` expectations updated. TEST: hunt repro `TestHuntRestoredDefiniteFailureRequeuesWithoutWalletCheck` passing.
- **A-54 (ACCEPT, code fix)**: with a configured-but-unavailable provider, `enqueuePayout` uses its configured threshold for counting and holding; disabled providers keep "credited only"; notice copy says "as last recorded by the market". TEST: hunt repro `TestHuntAnnouncedDepositDroppedWhenCompletedWhileWalletUnavailable` passing.
- **A-47 (MODIFY, bug hunt #1)**. DISSENT: the verifier would also require a PGP proof before enrolling TOTP on a PGP-only account; rejected for now because the existing rule already accepts the password alone to turn PGP sign-in *off* on such an account, so password-only enabling is no weaker (revisit with A-32). ACCEPTANCE: when the account has any second factor enrolled, enabling another (`/pgp/2fa` enable, `/totp/activate`) and changing the saved PGP key require `confirmedTx(c, true, …)` — current password plus a current TOTP code when TOTP is enrolled (recovery codes are not accepted here, as in other `confirmedTx(c,true)` flows; a user without an authenticator turns TOTP off with a recovery code first — implementer's reading adopted); forms render `confirm-fields`; an account with no factor keeps today's flow; audit rows say how it was confirmed. TEST: the hunt's `TestHuntStolenSessionAddsPGPFactorBypassingTOTP` and `TestHuntStolenSessionEnrollsTOTPBypassingPGP` moved into `auth_gaps_test.go` and passing; existing TOTP/PGP Playwright specs updated.
- **A-48 (ACCEPT, P1 — staying signed in is a headline workflow on a market reached through links)**. Verifier ruling adopted: keep `session` SameSite=Strict (Lax would let any site probe sign-in state and fire side-effecting GETs such as the one-time recovery-code reveal). ACCEPTANCE: pre-login state (CSRF/CAPTCHA token) lives in a separately named Strict cookie; a cookieless request never sets or overwrites `session`; `session` is set only at sign-in (token still rotated); a form rendered on a cookieless landing page still submits (accept the anonymous token for CSRF, or re-render once); after a cross-site link click, a direct reload of /account is still signed in; login, register, CAPTCHA and CSRF behave as before. TEST: the hunt's `TestHuntCrossSiteLinkClobbersSessionCookie` passing; `TestLoginRotatesSessionToken` unchanged; a db-*.spec.ts cross-site navigation case (routed foreign origin) in Chromium.
- **A-13 (AMENDED by DA-2, QA3-3, PM-5)**: add an evidence table to `docs/verification.md` (check, tier, commit SHA, CI run or log path, date); record QA's 509356d real-chain run; tick `acceptance.md` lines only against a row (CI-backed lines: the green CI run at that SHA; manual tiers: their own row); remove the contradictory "not run"/"passed" sentences; the original A-13 drift items stay. A chain-journey rerun is required after watcher/adapter changes (A-7, A-23) before any release (QA MISSED). **Done for A-7**: chain-journey (bitcoind 31.1 regtest + monerod 0.18.5.1 offline stagenet) 1/1 and regtest-smoke PASS on 699ea45 (QA-reported; the SHA-pinned logs were not retained — DA9-8, verification.md); **Done after A-23/A-52**: chain-journey 1/1 (4 real payouts, none ambiguous) and regtest-smoke PASS on cdb69f2 (QA-reported, binaries' SHA-256 re-checked; logs not retained — DA9-8). Superseded for release by A-103.
- **A-12 → P2** (DA-9): CI already sets `TEST_DATABASE_URL` and an unreachable URL fails; keep the shared-admin sign-in budget, the TOTP boundary and QA3-7's click-then-reload races.
- **A-14, A-19 → Declined** (DA-8, DA-10). **PM-5** (publish a prerelease tag) → owner action after P0/P1 close (A-46, blocked on owner; listed under P3 for tracking).

### Iteration 5 rulings (chair, 2026-09-24) — from bug hunt #2 verified findings

- **A-66 (ACCEPT, P0)**. WHY: after either documented recovery (side-by-side restore, UPGRADING rollback) every routine backup silently omits live data; verified on a real internal-db Compose stack. ACCEPTANCE: `scripts/backup.sh` (internal-db path) dumps the database named in `DATABASE_URL` from `.env` (path component; never printed with credentials), overridable by `BACKUP_INTERNAL_DATABASE`; if both are set and differ it refuses with a clear message; the success line names the database dumped; `scripts/restore.sh` prints which database it restored into and the exact `.env` change plus that backups now follow `DATABASE_URL`; operator-guide and UPGRADING say so, and warn external-db operators to point `BACKUP_DATABASE_URL` at the database they switched to. TEST: new case in `scripts/test-internal-db-restore.sh` — restore side by side, switch `DATABASE_URL`, write a row, `backup.sh`, restore that backup into a check database, assert the row is present; plus the refusal case; `bash -n`; the script is in CI's operations job.
- **A-67 (ACCEPT, P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: `/messages` accepts exactly one armored PGP MESSAGE block and nothing but whitespace outside it; decodes the whole base64 body; accepts only key packets (PKESK/SKESK) followed by exactly one encrypted-data packet (SEIPD or AEAD), read to EOF, with no trailing packets; stores a canonical re-armor of the decoded bytes (armor headers dropped), so no plaintext from headers, a second block or text around the block is stored or shown; real gpg/go-crypto output (Version/Comment headers, CRLF, multiple and hidden recipients) is still accepted with the same encryption-status badges. TEST: the hunt repro `TestHuntMessagePlaintextInsideArmorBadgedEncrypted` (three variants) plus the two sandwich variants (block+text+block, block+text+bare END) → 400, nothing stored; acceptance cases with CRLF, Comment header and two recipients → 303 and the stored body contains no header text; existing message/PGP tests and `db-pgp-messages.spec.ts` pass.
- Chair note (A-67): the Comment-header variant is accepted with the header dropped (303, nothing stored), per the acceptance text; the test line's "→ 400" for it was inconsistent. Pre-fix stored rows are re-checked on display and lose the Encrypted badge if they fail.
- **A-72 (ACCEPT code fix, P1)**. DISSENT: the verifier noted a docs-only fix (admins may read/replace auto-delivery content) is defensible; rejected because the A-24 ruling made delivery secrecy from non-party staff deliberate, and admin substitution under the vendor's name is the stronger harm. ACCEPTANCE: `/listing-edit` never loads or renders `delivery_content` for an editor who is not the listing's vendor (shows "Automatic delivery content is set; only the vendor can view or change it", or "not set"); `listingUpdateAction` keeps the stored content when the editor is not the vendor, whatever the form sends; a non-vendor admin changing the kind to physical while content is stored is refused as for the vendor; the vendor's editor is unchanged; docs (implementation-status P4 "Administrators may edit any listing", operator-guide) state the exception. TEST: the verifier's `artifacts/bug-hunt/cdb69f2/verify/batch-c/verify_b2c_test.go` adapted into `inventory_integration_test.go` (admin page hides the secret; admin title save keeps it; admin POST with different content does not change what the next order receives; vendor still sees and edits); a `db-inventory.spec.ts` admin assertion if cheap (fresh accounts).

### Iteration 6 rulings (chair, 2026-09-24) — original-audit P1s given acceptance criteria

- **A-8 (ACCEPT)**. ACCEPTANCE: opening a dispute notifies every moderator and administrator who is not a party to the order (link-free text naming the order, as other notifications); if no eligible resolver exists (every staff account is a party), the dispute is still accepted — the buyer's protection is never refused — and every administrator is notified that the dispute needs a moderator who is not a party, and the order history records that no independent resolver was available; the moderator desk shows such disputes with an "no eligible resolver" note to admins. TEST: Go DB — dispute on an order with a separate moderator notifies the moderator and admin but not the parties; order whose vendor is the only admin and with no moderator: dispute 303, admin notified with the no-resolver text, history note written.
- **A-9 (ACCEPT)**. ACCEPTANCE: startup no longer shares one 15 s context across ping, advisory lock, migrations and payment init: the database ping and lock acquisition keep bounded timeouts (configurable, default ≥ 15 s), migrations run under their own longer bound (default several minutes, configurable, documented) so a slow migration on a large database does not crash-loop; payment init keeps its own bound; timeouts produce an error naming the phase. TEST: Go unit/DB tests with a tiny migration bound showing the phase-named error, and a normal start unchanged; docs (operator-guide startup/upgrade) name the setting.
- **A-10 (ACCEPT)**. ACCEPTANCE: sealed plaintext recovery codes (`users.recovery_reveal`) never outlive their 10-minute window: expired reveals are cleared by a periodic background sweep (registered with `registerBackground`, cancellable on shutdown; at startup and every minute) and when the account next opens a security page (/account, /pgp, /admin, /totp) — amended after implementation: sign-in-time clearing was not built and adds no exposure given the one-minute sweep; docs (implementation-status recovery codes) state exactly how long sealed codes can exist; nothing changes for a reveal still within its window. TEST: Go DB — a reveal older than its window is cleared by one sweep; an in-window reveal survives; shutdown stops the sweep.
- **A-11 (ACCEPT)**. ACCEPTANCE: a limiter entry for sign-in is created or incremented only after the CAPTCHA passes (when CAPTCHA is on) and only for a well-formed handle; when the in-memory table is full, eviction never removes an entry that is currently blocking (count ≥ its limit) — prefer the lowest-count non-blocking entry; if every entry is blocking, new keys are refused with 429 rather than evicting; docs (implementation-status sign-in challenge / limiter text) match. TEST: Go unit tests on `allow` (full table of blocking entries → new key refused, blocking entry never evicted; junk keys cannot reset a victim's counter) and a DB test that a wrong CAPTCHA does not consume a handle's sign-in budget.

### Iteration 9 rulings (chair, 2026-09-24) — privacy sweep (privacy, security, frontend, buyer; Rounds A–C)

No blocker: every seat confirmed the narrow promises hold on real responses (no IP/user-agent capture or access log; CSP `default-src 'none'`; 348 requests from a Safest-like Firefox through signed-in DB flows, all same-origin; no secrets in logs; headers on every response class). The failures are what the product republishes or invites users to store, and what it never discloses.

- **A-79 (ACCEPT, P1; merges PRIV9-2, SEC9-1, FE9-2, BUY9-2, FE9-5)**. SEC9-1's confirm checkbox for "@" user IDs REJECTED — withdrawn by security itself: it fires on nearly every GnuPG key (email is the default), cannot be judged reliably, and without JavaScript costs a refused round-trip that loses the pasted key in Firefox (A-59). Closing the handle lookup rejected (first contact needs it). ACCEPTANCE: `saveProfileKey` stores a canonical re-armor of the parsed key (no text outside the block, no armor headers; key packets unchanged; fingerprint and existing ownership verification survive); legacy rows are shown canonically at display time on every page (A-74 pattern), no rewrite migration; /account lists the saved key's user IDs next to the fingerprint and, above the key field, says who can see it ("Shown to every signed-in account that looks up your handle, to every vendor or buyer you trade with, and publicly on your vendor page if you sell — with its user IDs and creation date. Use a key made only for this market."); other users see "Ownership verified YYYY-MM-DD" (date only), the owner's /account and /pgp keep the minute; implementation-status (key display, line ~42) states that `/messages?to=` shows the key to any signed-in account. Note (security caveat): re-armor does not remove user IDs, creation time or third-party signatures — the disclosure is what closes it. TEST: Go DB `TestProfileKeyStoredCanonically` in `pgp_integration_test.go` (key with leading text + Comment/Version headers → stored value and anonymous /vendor and a second account's /messages?to= contain neither; fingerprint unchanged; /account lists the user ID; others see a date without HH:MM); legacy-row display case; `db-pgp-messages.spec.ts` (fresh account) asserts the user-ID list.
- **A-80 (ACCEPT, P1 copy-only; merges PRIV9-3, SEC9-2, FE9-3, BUY9-1, BUY9-3, PRIV9-11)**. ACCEPTANCE: help text on the vendor's shipping note (and rename it "Note to the buyer"), the cancel reason, both dispute-reason forms (`order.html` and `disputes.html`) and the review body stating that the text is stored unencrypted, who can read it (the other party; staff when disputed or flagged; the operator and backups) and never to include an address, real name or tracking number — send those encrypted from the order page (dispute forms point to the encrypted "Contact dispute staff" keys); review help adds "The vendor sees your review, with your handle, on this order."; `implementation-status.md:57,59` and `operator-guide.md:104` qualified accordingly ("no address field; the market cannot stop addresses typed into plaintext fields; the encrypted address message is kept"). TEST: `preview-orders.spec.ts` assertions on the ship/cancel/dispute/review help; `TestReviewsRequireACompletedOrder` pins that the vendor's /order shows the review body.
- **A-81 (ACCEPT, P1; merges PRIV9-1, BUY9-4, FE9-4, SEC9-3 + PRIV9-5 export framing, BUY MISSED)**. SEC9-3 revised to accept a docs/copy fix (no export code change, invariant 8). ACCEPTANCE: `/canary` gains `<section id="records">` (h2 + list per data class, no table — 320 px) naming each stored class a user creates (handle, password hash, sealed TOTP, PGP key, messages as ciphertext plus sender/recipient/time, notifications, orders and drafts incl. cancelled, order notes, disputes, reviews, deliveries, payment addresses and txids, payout addresses, audit rows incl. sign-ins), who can read it (parties, staff on disputed/flagged orders, administrators, the operator and backups) and "kept until the operator removes it; there is no account or message deletion"; states that the application records no IP address or browser details (a reverse proxy may); says the signed audit export names every account's activity and is private operator evidence, never to be published, and /canary no longer implies visitors receive exports (admin export panel and operator-guide say the same); linked from /register, /account and the /messages notice; "YOUR PRIVACY. YOUR CHOICE." replaced by one factual line linking `/canary#records` (rendered once per viewport; screen readers not told twice); shipping line on order/vendor pages reads that the market keeps the encrypted message and who sent it to whom and when; /register and /setup say the handle cannot be changed, is shown to trading partners and staff, and should not be reused from elsewhere; implementation-status carries the same list, checked against the schema. TEST: preview spec on `/canary#records` heading and the three links; `routes_test.go` asserts no template contains "YOUR CHOICE".
- **A-82 (ACCEPT, P1; FE9-1, supported by security and privacy)**. ACCEPTANCE: every application database connection runs with `TimeZone=UTC` (pgx runtime parameter on the pool, not a database-level setting the operator may not own); every displayed timestamp (orders, history, messages, notifications, audit, disputes, payouts, provider polls, listings) is labelled " UTC"; with the database's `TimeZone` set to e.g. `Pacific/Chatham`, rendered times equal `now() AT TIME ZONE 'UTC'`. TEST: Go DB via `newTestApp` with `ALTER DATABASE … SET timezone='Pacific/Chatham'` asserting /order and /messages times; `db-orders.spec.ts` asserts the " UTC" suffix.
- **A-83 (MODIFY, P1; BUY9-5)**. DISSENT: security wanted a vendor-side publish gate (a physical listing cannot be published or restored without a usable encryption key) — DEFERRED to A-91 because it changes vendor onboarding and existing listings; the buyer-side check covers the harm now. Warning-plus-confirmation rejected (the key can change between draft and pay; trains click-through). ACCEPTANCE: A-68's check ("vendor has a key that can receive encrypted messages": not expired, has an encryption subkey) is implemented once and used for both: A-68's "No usable key" contact note, and physical orders — the order page replaces the pay form with "Unavailable: this vendor has no usable encryption key, so you could not send your shipping address. Ask them to add one." (existing "Unavailable. <reason>" pattern), product and checkout pages show the note, `/orders/pay` refuses with 409 as a backstop before any reservation or `NewAddress` call; digital orders unaffected; a paid physical order whose vendor key stopped being usable shows a "no usable key — message the vendor or open a dispute" state instead of the send-address prompt. Closes A-68. TEST: Go DB via `newPayEnv` — keyless and expired-key vendor, physical → 409, fake provider `NewAddress` not called, nothing reserved; digital → 303; A-68's `TestHuntUnusableEncryptionKeyShownAsUsableContact` passing.

### Iteration 9 rulings (chair, 2026-09-24) — payments sweep (payments, qa-release, staff, devils-advocate; Rounds A–C)

No second send for any order was found (QA: `go test -race` 259/0, wallet journey 4/4, chain-journey 1/1 on real local chains with 4 real payouts; DA: 21 mutations, the watcher's mechanics mostly pinned). The conflict-hold invariant, however, is bypassable, and the human wallet check that ends every anti-double-pay path is documented with commands that can miss the send.

- **A-97 (P0; PAY9-1 + DA9 MISSED + A-64 revised + DA9-7)**. DISSENT: devils-advocate rates it P1 (the trigger needs a post-threshold reversal >24 h after close on a payout still unsent; the loss is the pool's, not a second payment) — overruled on the invariant and the A-52 precedent (a payout that includes a deposit the ledger would mark conflicted breaks the hold invariant), with QA's real-chain observation that reorged deposits sit at depth 0 and the common blocked-refund path; payments and QA support P0. Fix shape: DA's narrower watch set adopted over "watch every unsent payout forever" (unbounded polling of blocked/failed rows that cannot send until a person acts). ACCEPTANCE: (1) the watch set also includes every order with a payout in `pending`, or `held` by the watcher (`error` = the watcher's hold reason), with no age limit; (2) `sendPayouts` claims a payout only for an order whose addresses were read **and** whose `settleOrder` succeeded in this pass (e.g. `AND order_id = ANY($settledOK)`); a payout that becomes pending mid-pass (address saved, admin release/requeue) waits for the next pass; fresh completions are still sent on the next pass; (3) a watcher-held payout whose deposit re-confirms is un-held regardless of address age; (4) the claim re-check at :510 and the `recordPayout` state CAS (:586) are pinned by tests. TEST (Go DB, `payments_watcher_gaps_test.go`, newPayEnv + fake): QA's `qa9_bc_probe_test.go.txt` and the payments probe as named regressions — completed order `updated` 48 h ago, blocked release, wallet drops the deposit, address saved → flagged, held, 0 sends; `TestPendingPayoutNotSentWhenAddressUnread`; `TestSendSkipsOrderWhoseSettleFailed`; `TestOldHeldPayoutResumesAfterReconfirm` (address 40 days old); a claim-recheck case that fails if :510 is removed; `TestLateRecordDoesNotOverwriteAdminResolution` (fails if :586's `state='sending'` is removed); existing lifecycle and A-52 race tests pass under `-race`.
- **A-98 (ACCEPT, P1; QA9-1 + STAFF9-6 + STAFF9-2 + A-31's truncation part + QA MISSED)**. DISSENT: staff wanted A-31 (web disposition) raised to P1 before any new flag source — MODIFIED: its load-bearing part (undisposed flags never hidden, reason from current state) moves here; the web "refunded manually (txid)" disposition stays P2 (A-31) because the refund itself needs the wallet shell and a hijacked moderator session recording a refund is the D-6 objection (DA). ACCEPTANCE: (1) a credited deposit that falls below the threshold (confirmations ≥ 0) while its order is non-terminal or its payout is unsent is announced once per episode — one order event, notifications to buyer, vendor and every non-party moderator/admin — and makes the order staff-readable and listed for payment review (via `payments.flagged` or an equivalent marker the loaders use); re-confirming ends the episode and a later regression or conflict is announced again (no `AND NOT flagged` suppression across episodes); a completed order whose payout was already sent is not flagged; (2) no announcement while the node's tip is below the highest tip the watcher has recorded for that currency (store it in `payment_status`; a lower tip counts as syncing and the pass is skipped) — so a node crash that rolls the tip back raises no false alarms; (3) /moderator's payment-review list shows every open (unhandled) flag first, oldest first and uncapped, then recent others with a count, and each row's reason comes from the deposit's current confirmations ("conflicted", "below threshold (N)", "confirmed again"); (4) /admin shows no Release form for a watcher-held payout whose deposit is still below threshold — it names the deposit, its confirmations and the address and links to the (now staff-readable) order; the 409 text and `heldReason` no longer promise a moderator review. TEST: one table test in `payments_watcher_gaps_test.go` (order fresh / `updated` 48 h / address 40 d; payout blocked / pending / restored-held; sequence drop → 0 confs → recover → drop again → tip lowered; assert per-episode event and notification counts, held, sends 0 until recovery then exactly 1); `payment_review_integration_test.go` desk ordering with 1 old open flag + 101 newer; admin page has no Release form for the watcher-held row; `TestPayoutHeldWhileCreditedDepositReconfirms` still passes. Ships after A-97 (same files).
- **A-99 (MODIFY, P1; DA9-1)**. Payments and QA: a floor does not make every send succeed (fees move; requeue stays right above it) — it removes the never-sendable class; staff: flagging must set the marker the desk and staff read use and must not re-flag every pass. ACCEPTANCE: a BTC minimum payout constant (10,000 sat, documented) and an XMR minimum automatic-refund amount set on fee economics (documented); listing validation refuses a BTC price below the floor (400); `enqueuePayout` inserts no payout row when the sum is below the currency's floor and instead flags the order once (appears on the payment-review list, staff can read it, no repeat on later passes); implementation-status "Late deposits" states the exception; an above-floor payout behaves exactly as today. TEST: `TestDustBTCRefundIsFlaggedNotQueued` (fake deposit 1,000 sat, cancel, two passes → no payout, one flag note, one staff notification); inventory test: listing below floor → 400. Ships with or after A-98 (flag path).
- **A-100 (ACCEPT, P1; DA9-3)**. ACCEPTANCE: the node and wallet images in `compose.nodes.yaml` / `.env.example` default to pinned release tags matching the verified versions (bitcoind 31.1, monerod/monero-wallet-rpc 0.18.5.1), overridable in `.env`; operator-guide and implementation-status P5 state that the Monero "nothing broadcast" table holds for monero-wallet-rpc v0.18.5.1 and give a bump checklist (re-verify `moneroPreSubmitCodes` against the new wallet source before changing the tag; with an unverified version, treat every Monero send failure as possibly broadcast); CI's Compose job asserts no `:latest` for node/wallet images. TEST: the Compose configuration check; `actionlint`; docs validated.
- **A-101 (ACCEPT, P1; DA9-4, PAY9-2 folded, DA9-2, STAFF9-4)**. PAY9-2's minimum requeue delay RETRACTED by payments after reading the v0.18.5.1 source (a pool spend becomes pending on the next 20 s refresh; wallet-rpc serves one request at a time) — the runbook fix closes it more cheaply. ACCEPTANCE: `testnet-runbook.md` recovery checks use `listsinceblock <hash before the failure>` (or `listtransactions "*" 100000`) for Bitcoin and, for Monero, `refresh` then `get_transfers` twice, treating any pending/out transfer created after the failure without destinations as this payout until proven otherwise; the admin confirmation hint says the same in one line; operator-guide "Backups and recovery", the runbook reconcile section and UPGRADING §6 warn that any restore not done by `scripts/restore.sh` skips the payout protection and give the §6 SQL to run before starting the app; `restore.sh`, the §6 SQL and the step-8 clear SQL write an audit row (gate set by restore / set manually / cleared by `<admin handle>`), refusing an unknown handle. TEST: on regtest, the documented BTC command finds a payout with >50 later wallet entries (recorded log); `scripts/test-backup-restore.sh` case copying the DB with plain `pg_dump`/`pg_restore`, applying the §6 SQL, asserting the gate, holds and the audit row; `bash -n`.
- **A-102 (ACCEPT, P1; payments MISSED)**. ACCEPTANCE: saving a payout address notifies the account owner (link-free, naming the currency, not the address); A-27's suspend also holds the account's `pending` and `blocked` payouts (`held`, error prefixed `Suspended account:`) and restore does not release them automatically; an admin releases such a payout only with `confirmedTx` and a "payout address checked" confirmation, audited; the watcher never un-holds them. TEST: Go DB — vendor with a blocked release changes address → notification; admin suspends → payout held, 0 sends across passes; admin release without the confirmation → 409, with it → pending; restore leaves it held.
- **A-103 (P1, release evidence)**: run by `opsec-qa-release` at the end of this loop on the final SHA.
- **DA9-8**: the ledger's real-chain wording for A-7, A-52 and the A-13 ruling now says the logs were not retained (done in this commit).

### Iteration 10 rulings (chair, 2026-09-24) — UI & accessibility sweep (frontend, buyer, vendor, product-completeness; Rounds A–C)

No blocker survived cross-examination: BUY10-1 was revised to REQUIRED by its author after frontend showed the amount is reachable by panning. The no-JS design holds (every task reachable by keyboard in Firefox; text contrast ≥ 7:1 on all 21 preview routes except archived-card art at 3.45:1); the failures are in what the preview cannot show (real 64-hex IDs, deposits, admin payout rows, long handles) and in the recipient side of money and moderation.

- **A-115 (ACCEPT, P1, first in the UI wave)**. ACCEPTANCE: `.grid-two` tracks `minmax(0,1fr)` (mobile and desktop); `overflow-wrap:anywhere` on page-head mono text, breadcrumbs, `.account-name` and page-head h1s (or a short ID with the full ID in a wrappable block); tables scroll inside their `.table-wrap`; document scrollWidth ≤ viewport on real orders (64-hex IDs and txids) at 320, 390 and 1280 in draft, partial, paid and completed states, on /disputes and /moderator, and on /vendor with a 32-character handle at 100% and 200% text. TEST: wallet-journey width assertions after the partial deposit and after completion (390 + a 320 context); `db-orders.spec.ts` draft width; preview order uses a 64-hex ID and one sample deposit row; the 200% loop covers every preview route.
- **A-116 (ACCEPT, P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: payout resolve forms wrap inside their cell (`white-space:normal` for `.payout-actions` and `td.mono`, sensible min-width); at 1280 px with a form open every help text and checkbox label is fully visible when its control has focus; the summary names the payout, amount, recipient and order ("Resolve payout 7: refund 0.001 BTC to <handle>, order 2d6b549f"). TEST: `db-admin.spec.ts` (fresh accounts) with a seeded held/ambiguous payout: label box inside `.table-wrap` at 1280, computed white-space normal.
- **A-117 (ACCEPT, P1)**. DISSENT: buyer (keep a URL flag naming the action, accept the Back artefact) — rejected: a URL flag is replayed by Back/history under `no-store` (frontend measured it) and a crafted link can show a false success. ACCEPTANCE: a one-shot notice cookie (HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, holding an enum code from a fixed server table, never user text) set only on the 303 of a successful signed-in POST (never on the cookieless landing path — A-48) and cleared on first render; `?saved=1` removed; specific texts ("Password changed.", "Message sent.", "Payout address saved.", "<handle> suspended.", "Signed out." …); rendered inside the targeted section or at the top without a fragment; `<title>` prefixed with the notice, and with "Error: " when `d.Error` is set; object titles for product/order/vendor pages and "Transparency"/"Marketplace" for /canary and /. TEST: `db-identity.spec.ts` save → failed password change → Back shows no success notice; reload shows it at most once; `db-admin.spec.ts` suspend → notice in viewport and title prefixed; Go unit test for the cookie round trip.
- **A-118 (ACCEPT, P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: the resolver/notification query (`actions_orders.go` ~:80/89), `NoResolver` (`orders_load.go` ~:296), dispute staff contacts (`contacts.go` ~:62) and `flagOrder`'s recipients exclude suspended accounts; with the only non-party moderator suspended the no-resolver note, admin notice and desk note appear, contacts omit the suspended account; restoring reverses it; the admin suspend copy and operator-guide no longer say orders continue normally ("orders waiting on this account stop until it is restored; the other party can still complete, cancel where allowed, or open a dispute"). TEST: `TestDisputeIgnoresSuspendedStaff` (dispute_staff_integration_test.go), a flag case in payment_review_integration_test.go, preview-auth copy assertion.
- **A-119 (ACCEPT, P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: one administrator setting "Staff contact" (free text: a handle, onion address or XMPP; password-confirmed, audited old→new) rendered on /canary, in the suspended sign-in refusal, as a "Lost your authenticator and recovery codes?" line on /challenge, under the payout-address change notice, and as "Can't read the image?" help on /login and /register; when a staff member has a key usable for encryption, a `/messages?to=<handle>` link is added; unset → "Ask the operator of this market" (no page implies an in-app channel that does not exist). TEST: Go DB — the refusal body contains the configured contact; preview-auth `/challenge` lost-factors line; setting change audited.
- **A-120 (ACCEPT, P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: `/account#payout` lists every payout to the viewer that is not sent (blocked, held, pending, sending, failed), uncapped, oldest first, with order link, amount, a per-state reason and next step in fixed copy (never raw wallet errors): blocked → save an address; failed-definite → "the market wallet refused the send before broadcasting; administrators have been notified"; failed-ambiguous → "may have been broadcast; an administrator checks the wallet before any resend"; held (watcher / restore / suspension) → neutral reason; the vendor desk shows one count linking there; `/order` replaces "No further changes are possible" with the payout's next step when an unsent payout exists for the viewer; the payout's destination is shown to its recipient only (A-84); suspension/restore notices say how many payouts are held; no order events on address save (N rows per save — PM). Folds A-107's neutral label, A-112's label and notification parts, and QA9-6/DA9-6. TEST: Go DB with blocked/held/failed payouts on orders older than 101 newer ones → all listed; recipient-only destination; wallet-journey refund leg links to `/account#payout`.
- **A-121 (MODIFY, P1)**. VEN10-1's automatic re-point on address save REJECTED (payments/buyer: a stolen password re-points; definite-failed requeue is gated only by the admin's password). ACCEPTANCE: an admin action "use the account's current address" on a held or definite-failed payout, behind `confirmedTx` and the `address_checked` confirmation, showing the payout's address and the account's current saved address (and when it last changed); ambiguous-failed, sending and restore-held payouts are never re-pointed; saving an address notifies "N unsent payouts still use your previous address; an administrator must confirm the change", naming the orders; audit records old→new. TEST: `TestAdminMovesHeldPayoutToCurrentAddress`, `TestAddressSaveDoesNotRepointUnsentPayouts`, and a pin for a suspension hold that already has an address when the owner saves a new one.
- **A-122 (MODIFY, P1)**. One shape for BUY10-2 + FE10-7: ACCEPTANCE: the complete form shows the exact release amount and recipient and carries a `required` checkbox whose label states them ("Release 0.005 BTC (test network) to <vendor> — 0.0045 more than the price. This is final."); the server refuses a missing field (400, no state change); the vendor's cancel of a paid order carries the same with the refund amount; when received > required both parties' order pages show an alert ("the buyer sent X more than required; the whole amount goes to the vendor on completion — message the vendor or open a dispute before completing"; the vendor's copy notes that cancelling refunds every deposit); the release notification names any excess; implementation-status states the rule. No automatic refund of the excess (invariant 4). TEST: Go DB missing-field 400; overpaid order page alert; wallet-journey asserts the amount in the complete label.
- **A-123 (ACCEPT, P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: the awaiting-payment page shows "Send payment before YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM UTC. If no deposit has been seen by then, the order is cancelled." computed at render with the watcher's own `paymentExpiry()` and `payment_addresses.created` (no absolute time in notifications — PM); notifications for system/moderator transitions append the event note ("Cancelled — payment window expired", "Resolved — refund to buyer"). TEST: Go DB (newPayEnv) deadline on /order; backdated expiry notification contains the reason; resolve notification contains the outcome.
- **A-124 (ACCEPT, P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: on a disputed order the party who did not open the dispute may submit one reply (20–5000 characters, same A-80 warning copy, help points to encrypted staff messages first when a staff key exists), stored once as an order event, shown to both parties and on the moderator desk with its role, rendered `white-space:pre-wrap` (also fix the reason's line breaks); a second reply → 409; not offered after resolution; the dispute-opened notification tells the non-opener the reply exists; the resolve form shows whether the other side replied. TEST: `TestDisputeCounterpartyResponse`; db-orders dispute leg with fresh accounts.
- **A-125 (ACCEPT, P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: an administrator "Hide review" action (password-confirmed, audited, reviewer notified) that hides — never deletes — a review from /product and /vendor (new column in a numbered migration); the admin sees hidden reviews labelled; implementation-status and /canary#records say administrators can hide reviews. TEST: Go DB hidden review absent from public pages; audit row; non-admin 403.
- **A-126 (ACCEPT, P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: protected-page redirects go to `/login?next=<path>` where next is accepted only if it names a registered GET page (plus its `id` query) — never an action, `//`, a scheme or an absolute URL; carried through /register and the pending sign-in through /challenge; "Sign in to see Orders." copy on /login when next is set. TEST: Go DB table test incl. open-redirect cases; `db-auth.spec.ts` anonymous checkout → sign in (and TOTP) → `/checkout?id=X`.
- **A-127 (ACCEPT, P1 docs + dead code)**. ACCEPTANCE: `/admin` runs no `orderQuery`; implementation-status and A-16 no longer claim an admin order list (building one would reverse A-24 and the /canary#records promise — a new ruling, not a doc line). TEST: existing admin tests unchanged.
- **A-128 (ACCEPT, P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: `db-seeded-marketplace.spec.ts` waits for the navigation (`waitForURL` + `waitForLoadState`) before `reload()`; grep the DB specs for the same click → `url()` → `reload()` shape (A-12 lists `db-account.spec.ts:24-25`, `db-desktop.spec.ts:44-45`) and fix them the same way; no test weakened or skipped. TEST: the specs pass 10× serially locally with fresh accounts (mind the shared-admin sign-in budget).

### Iteration 10 rulings (chair, 2026-09-24) — operations sweep (infrastructure, staff, qa-release, devils-advocate; Rounds A–C)

No blocker: QA ran the operator journeys end to end at e538601 — first onion evidence over public Tor (Tor + internal DB), Tor + external DB with the egress overlay, restart during a payout (SIGTERM and SIGKILL; one wallet call, never re-sent), DB outage recovery without restart, backup onto a full disk (clean refusal), side-by-side restore and the UPGRADING §5 rollback at binary tier. The failures are what CI never runs (Tor at runtime, Compose-level upgrade, a second checkout), what backup.sh does not capture, and what the operator cannot see (no error logs).

- **A-134 (ACCEPT, P1, first in the ops wave)**. Pinned container IP REJECTED (DA/infrastructure: subnet collisions, bitcoind `rpcallowip`, forces a network recreate); staff's "must be automatic" satisfied by `restart: true`. ACCEPTANCE: `tor` and `tor-mirror` use `depends_on: {app: {condition: service_started, restart: true}}` (Compose ≥ 2.17 documented); UPGRADING §4 and the operator guide add a one-time `docker compose up -d --force-recreate tor tor-mirror` for existing Tor installs (QA: the new depends_on does not heal an already-broken install) and "if the onion does not answer after any change, recreate tor"; CI's Compose job asserts `restart: true` on app for both tor services from `config --format json`. TEST: that CI assertion; a local churn check recorded in verification.md (tor StartedAt changes when app is recreated; QA's onion harness optional).
- **A-135 (ACCEPT, P1)**. Renaming existing installs REJECTED (they would start on new empty volumes). ACCEPTANCE: `install.sh` refuses when containers labelled `com.docker.compose.project=opsecmkt` (or the configured project) exist with a different `com.docker.compose.project.working_dir`, naming the other directory and how to proceed; new installs may set `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` explicitly in `.env` (default stays `opsecmkt` for compatibility); README's "`APP_PORT=8081` if 8080 is occupied" line and operator-guide "rehearse recovery" say to use a distinct `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` (e.g. `opsecmkt-rehearsal`) for any second checkout on the same host. TEST: `tests/test_installer.py` case with a stubbed `docker ps` reporting a foreign working_dir → refused; `scripts/test-install.py` second-install case asserts the first install's database survives.
- **A-136 (ACCEPT, P1)**. Shape: documented commands, no new script (infrastructure B-round). ACCEPTANCE: operator-guide "Backups and recovery" gives exact, marker-delimited (`<!-- ops-cmd: … -->`) commands to back up `tor_identity` / `tor_mirror_identity` (tar through the Tor image, piped to `age -r "$AGE_RECIPIENT"`) and `.env` (age-encrypted copy), and to restore them (refuse a non-empty target; extract with `--user 0`; `chown -R debian-tor:debian-tor` and `chmod 700`; print the hostname to compare); a short secret inventory (SETUP_TOKEN, AUDIT_SIGNING_KEY, POSTGRES_PASSWORD with `ALTER ROLE` rotation, RPC passwords, onion keys) with backup and rotation notes; `restore.sh`'s last line names SETUP_TOKEN and AUDIT_SIGNING_KEY. TEST: `scripts/test-tor-identity-restore.sh` extracts the marked commands and runs them verbatim offline (`--network none`) in the tor-image CI job: backup → volume rm → restore → identical hostname, Tor starts; a root-owned target case.
- **A-137 (ACCEPT, P1)**. DA's condition adopted. ACCEPTANCE: `opsecmkt -reset-admin-factors ADMIN_HANDLE` beside `-reset-admin-password` (A-28's harness): one transaction clearing TOTP, recovery codes and PGP sign-in, ending sessions and pending sign-ins, one audit row naming the operator reset; refuses non-admins, unknown handles and a database from a newer release; operator-guide and UPGRADING replace **both** SQL recipes with the `docker compose run --rm app -reset-admin-factors HANDLE` command in the same change (non-admin cases point to the web reset). TEST: Go DB beside `admin_password_test.go` and a CLI subprocess test in `cmd/server/reset_admin_test.go`.
- **A-138 (ACCEPT, P1)**. Trusting `X-Forwarded-Host` or a `PUBLIC_ORIGIN` setting REJECTED. ACCEPTANCE: operator-guide states the proxy must forward the original Host header, with an nginx example (`proxy_set_header Host $host;`); the 403 text becomes fixed copy naming no header values ("Origin does not match Host; if this market runs behind a reverse proxy, the proxy must forward the Host header."); a mismatched Origin/Host still returns 403. TEST: Go test asserting 403 plus the text on a mismatch and 303 when they agree.
- **A-139 (MODIFY, P1)**. DISSENT: devils-advocate and infrastructure rated SHOULD (needs ~100k rows; fails safe) — overruled by QA's onion measurement (truncation at ~20k–50k rows over Tor). A ranged export (`from`) and a host export CLI are P2 (A-143). ACCEPTANCE: the export handler sets its own write deadline scaled to the export size (e.g. `http.NewResponseController(w).SetWriteDeadline`) and builds the export under its own bound instead of the 12 s request context; output bytes for a given `upto` unchanged (invariant 8); other routes keep the 20 s WriteTimeout; the admin export help says a partial download fails verification with "sha256 does not match". TEST: audit_export_test.go with a real `http.Server` (short WriteTimeout) and a slow client: full body and valid signature; byte-stability tests unchanged.
- **A-140 (ACCEPT, P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: every documented mirror command works with an installer-written Tor + internal-db `.env` (`docker compose up -d tor-mirror`, or add `mirror` to COMPOSE_PROFILES); runbook step 9 restarts the mirror when present; CI's Compose job adds an env-driven variant (COMPOSE_FILE/COMPOSE_PROFILES as the installer writes them) that runs the guide's mirror command with `--dry-run`. TEST: that CI step; `tests/test_installer.py` asserts the profile string.
- **A-141 (MODIFY, P1)**. DA's field restriction adopted; staff's /admin error ring deferred to A-143. ACCEPTANCE: one log line per 5xx — `http 5xx kind=page|action name=<registered route key> status=<code> ref=<8 hex> cause=<SQLSTATE[ table constraint] | timeout | canceled | db-connection | template:<name> | %T>` — never `err.Error()` text, IP, Host, User-Agent, URL path or query, cookies, handle/user id or form values; the same ref appears in the error body; each payout outcome (payout id, order short id, currency, amount, txid or error class) is logged in `sendPayouts` whether or not the watcher is shutting down (QA10-5, A-107 txid part); the startup database error names its class (timeout, host not found, refused, TLS required, authentication failed 28P01/28000, database missing 3D000) without the DSN. TEST: Go DB forcing a page and an action 500 with a handle in the query and a NUL in the form → one line each with the ref and none of the forbidden content (closes A-93's `TestLogsContainNoSecrets`); a cancelled-context payout-failure log case; `startup_test.go` cases for wrong password, unknown DB, unknown host.
- **A-142 (ACCEPT, P1 docs)**. ACCEPTANCE: runbook "Restore the wallets": Bitcoin `restorewallet` + rescan; Monero wallet files preferred, seed only as fallback with `restore_height` and a subaddress lookahead above the issued count (`SELECT count(*) FROM payment_addresses WHERE currency='XMR'`); then advance the address index past the database (`getnewaddress` / `create_address count=N`) before users return; placed in the reconcile sequence; `restore.sh`'s wallet reminder links it. TEST: docs validated; regtest variant in `scripts/regtest-smoke.sh` (backupwallet → issue addresses → restorewallet → the documented step prevents a collision) recorded as manual real-chain evidence; the Monero part settles with A-45.

### Owner directive (2026-09-24) — pruned local nodes

- **A-146 (OWNER DIRECTIVE, P1)**. DISSENT: infrastructure (iteration 10, NOT WORTH "recommending `bitcoind -prune`": it breaks `restorewallet` + rescan of an old backup) — overridden by the owner; the cost is carried in the runbook instead. ACCEPTANCE: `compose.nodes.yaml` runs `bitcoin` with `-prune=${BITCOIN_PRUNE_MB:-<default ≥ 550>}` (documented default sized for testnet4/signet; `-datadir=/data` added in the same command block — A-144/QA10-4) and `monero` with `--prune-blockchain` (and `--sync-pruned-blocks` if supported by the pinned version); nothing in the app requires an unpruned node (the adapters use wallet RPCs only — confirmed: `listreceivedbyaddress`, `gettransaction`, `sendtoaddress`, `listsinceblock`, monero-wallet-rpc); the interactive installer's per-coin prompt defaults to `local` ("local pruned node", default image and network as today) and states the disk and initial-sync cost, `external` and `disabled` remain; `--local` mode still disables nodes; operator-guide, README, testnet-runbook and UPGRADING say local nodes are pruned, give disk sizing, explain the restore trade-off (a Bitcoin wallet backup older than the prune window cannot be rescanned: restore with `-prune=0` temporarily / `-reindex`, or restore the wallet files; Monero wallets work with a pruned daemon) and what happens to an existing unpruned volume on upgrade (verify each daemon's behaviour on an existing full datadir from upstream docs/source; UNVERIFIED claims marked); A-142's wallet-restore section must include the pruning note. **Owner amendment (same day):** pruning is the default, not mandatory — `BITCOIN_PRUNE_MB=0` and `MONERO_PRUNE_FLAGS=''` give a full node, the installer asks per coin (default yes = pruned), Compose omits the prune flags for a full node; dev/testing/CI configurations, Tor-mirror and secondary/test instances keep the pruned default; docs cover switching (full→pruned prunes in place for bitcoind; pruned→full needs a resync) with upstream citations. TEST adds: CI check that `BITCOIN_PRUNE_MB=0`/`MONERO_PRUNE_FLAGS=''` yields no prune flags; installer test for answering `no`. TEST: CI Compose job asserts `-prune=` for bitcoin and `--prune-blockchain` for monero in the effective config; `tests/test_installer.py` asserts the default choice is local with pruned profiles and that `--local` stays disabled; `bash -n`; actionlint.

### Owner directive (2026-09-26) — DDoS hardening (Tier 1: A-176–A-181; Tier 2: A-182, A-183)

Source: DDoS assessment (Opus research: app surface, standard Tor/clearnet defences, academic literature incl. OnionFlation, USENIX Security 2025; Fable ranking). Tier 1 is authorised for implementation after the installer redesign (S1–S4) lands; Tier 2 stays open for a ruling.

- **A-176 (P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: GET `/login`, `/register` and `/captcha` perform no database write; the challenge id is `expiry ‖ nonce ‖ HMAC(key, expiry ‖ nonce ‖ session_hash)` and the answer stays derived from the id (`captchaAnswer`, captcha.go:42), so `answer_hash` is no longer needed; single use is enforced at POST by inserting the id into a spent-id table (primary key; conflict → 400), swept after expiry; PNG rendering is bounded by a semaphore (8) that returns 503 with `Retry-After`. Tests: 1 000 cookieless GET `/login` leave `captchas`/spent-table insert counts unchanged; a replayed id → 400; wrong session → 400; existing CAPTCHA tests pass.
- **A-177 (P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: `statement_timeout` 8 s, `lock_timeout` 3 s and `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` 15 s are set per connection for the APPLICATION pool only (pgx `RuntimeParams` beside `timezone` in `OpenDB`, app.go:121-134, behind an option), never for migrations or `-reset-admin-password`/`-bootstrap-admin`; the watcher's queries run under a bounded context. Tests: `pg_sleep(20)` on the app pool fails with SQLSTATE 57014 within the budget; a held row lock fails a competing UPDATE within 3 s; a migration runs with no statement timeout.
- **A-178 (P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: a local listener wrapper caps concurrent connections at 256 (no new module); per-class semaphores (pages 64, POST/DB writes 16, CAPTCHA PNG 8; bcrypt's 4 unchanged) refuse at once with 503 and `Retry-After`; every existing 503 and 429 carries `Retry-After`; 5xx log lines are throttled (at most 10/s, then one "suppressed N" line). Tests: under 500 concurrent searches from one client, a second client's `/healthz` p99 stays under 1 s and all refusals carry `Retry-After`; the log throttle is unit-tested. **RULING (iteration 17, chair):** the listener cap is 2048, not 256 (at 256 and a 5 s ReadHeaderTimeout about 51 trickling connections a second held every slot; the per-class semaphores bound the work, the cap bounds goroutines and buffers: 2048 × 64 KiB bodies stay inside GOMEMLIMIT), and "POST/DB writes 16" is 16 per class (signed-in, anonymous, post session lookup, taken after the body and checks) — both keep the criterion's intent (QA A-178 verification).
- **A-179 (P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: `deploy/torrc` (shared by `tor` and `tor-mirror`) sets `HiddenServicePoWDefensesEnabled 1`, `HiddenServicePoWQueueRate 60`, `HiddenServicePoWQueueBurst 600` (30/300 is the documented fallback without A-178), `HiddenServiceEnableIntroDoSDefense 1` at its 25/200 defaults (deliberately looser than the PoW queue), `HiddenServiceMaxStreams 32`, `HiddenServiceMaxStreamsCloseCircuit 1` and `HeartbeatPeriod 30 minutes`; `deploy/Dockerfile.tor` installs a pinned tor ≥ 0.4.8 from deb.torproject.org and the build fails unless `tor --list-modules` reports `pow: yes`; the CI Tor image job asserts both; the operator guide documents the knobs, the heartbeat lines to watch and the OnionFlation signature (onion probe failing while app CPU and pool are idle → raise QueueRate, do not lower it).
- **A-180 (P2)**. ACCEPTANCE: `mem_limit`/`cpus`/`pids_limit` on app (512m/2/512), tor and tor-mirror (256m/1/64) and db (1g), with node services sized in the operator guide; the CI compose job asserts them in `docker compose config --format json`; Compose `x-logging` rotation (A-144) lands with it.
- **A-181 (P2)**. ACCEPTANCE: `q` capped at 64 bytes with `%`/`_` escaped (closes A-60 PA-8); catalog rows render an excerpt of at most 300 characters; 25 results per page with paging (with A-16); index on `products(archived, created)`; a catalog page with 100 maximum-size listings renders under 100 KB. `pg_trgm` deferred (extension creation fails on locked-down external databases).
- **A-182 (P2, Tier 2 — ruling pending)**. Direction: second listener that requires a PROXY v1 line (source `fc00:dead:beef:4dad::/64` + circuit id) and refuses without one; circuit id kept only in request context, keyed into a separate bounded limiter (never A-75's table); ids never logged or persisted (extend `TestLogsContainNoSecrets`); Tor-mode CI proves the header path.
- **A-183 (P3, Tier 2 — ruling pending)**. Direction: staff address first; member address bucketed and rotated, never per-user (a per-user address is a tracking tag and a seized-server map).
- Tracked, not built: Tor prop 362 (PoW control loop, the real OnionFlation fix), prop 331 Res tokens, Onion Pass, Privacy Pass ARC/ACT (need a blinding client Tor Browser lacks), Onionbalance once PoW-compatible. Rejected: external CLI puzzle; CDN in front of the onion. A-160 (CAPTCHA machine solve rate) must be answered before any token scheme relies on the CAPTCHA.

### War room (2026-09-27) — image uploads + ClamAV (A-184–A-199, D-29–D-36)

**Preconditions.** (1) PR #15 (`ddos/integration`) merges to `main` before any implementer starts; every admission term below is PR #15's (`pageWork` 64, `anonPostWork`/`userPostWork`/`sessionLookupWork` 16, `captchaWork` 8, `passwordWork` 4, 64-field form cap, 2048-connection cap, app-pool timeouts 8 s/3 s/15 s). (2) A-110 (refuse PGP keys carrying user-attribute packets) is verified before or with this release, else "every upload scanned, no exceptions" is false on day one (DA-9). (3) Invariants: no browser JavaScript; test networks only; migrations append-only, next free **063**; the signed audit export stays byte-stable (new audit rows use fixed copy, no signature names, no digests).
**Standing design (undisputed, binding).** Same-origin only; JPEG+PNG in, JPEG out; original bytes never stored or served; type from magic bytes only; filename, part Content-Type and original size discarded; opaque 128-bit `randomToken()` ids; bytea in PostgreSQL (existing encrypted backup covers it); clamd on its own `scanner` network (`internal: true` in both modes, members app + clamd), hardened, pinned, no `depends_on`, `/healthz` independent; upload forms rendered only when `CLAMD_ADDRESS` is set, no skip switch; hard delete for replace, owner removal and staff takedown; moderators take down, never upload (owner Q1).

#### Decisions on disputed items (dissents kept)


##### C1/D2 — signature update path; image-only vs volume

- DECISION: **ACCEPT (image-only, both modes)**. clamd runs the pinned non-`_base` `clamav/clamav:<MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH>` image with **no signature volume**, reading the image layer's `/var/lib/clamav` read-only. Refresh = `install.sh refresh-scanner` (`docker compose pull clamd && docker compose up -d clamd`, prints the new `VERSION` date); `install.sh upgrade` runs it when the scanner profile is on; clearnet operators may cron it. Tor offline alternative: `docker save` the pinned tag elsewhere, `docker load` on the host. freshclam (volume + direct clearnet, or a dedicated Tor client) is opt-in only after A-195; enabling it replaces image signatures for good and the guide says so.
- WHY: SRE proved the seed trap (a named volume is populated only when empty, so a newer image never reaches clamd) and that `up --build` never re-pulls; no volume makes the trap impossible. The seats' own evidence predicts freshclam-over-Tor fails (CDN 403/429, UUID UA, no `HTTPUserAgent` on the CDN). A host pull is the egress class every upgrade already uses, operator-triggered, no container egress in Tor mode.
- DISSENTING VIEW: UI — a volume with a `VERSION` compare on upgrade/import, because image-only "silently ages". Kept as the fallback if A-195 shows upstream does not re-push version tags with newer signatures (UNVERIFIED; the implementer records two pulls of one tag a week apart or cites upstream docs).
- USER IMPACT: vendors see "paused" only when signatures pass the C2 gate.
- OPERATOR IMPACT: one new command; date on `/admin` and `install.sh status`.
- TECHNICAL COST/RISK: the official entrypoint chowns `/var/lib/clamav` (UNVERIFIED) → override `command: clamd --foreground --config-file=/etc/clamav/clamd.conf` as user `clamav`, tmpfs `/tmp` and `/run/clamav`; proven with `--read-only --network none` answering PING/VERSION.
- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: `docker compose config` shows clamd with no volume, `read_only: true`, networks `[scanner]` only; `refresh-scanner` re-pulls and recreates even when the tag is present and the `VERSION` date is ≥ before; no `freshclam` process in any default profile.
- TEST PLAN: `tests/test_installer.py::test_refresh_scanner_pulls_and_recreates`, `::test_upgrade_runs_refresh_scanner_only_when_profile_on`; `scripts/test-scanner.sh`: read-only + no-network PING/VERSION; no-volume clamd reports the image's daily date.

##### C2/D6 — staleness gate default and range

- DECISION: **MODIFY**. Gate kept; `SCANNER_MAX_SIGNATURE_AGE_DAYS` default **30**, range **7–90** (outside → startup refuses). Age = now − the daily date parsed from `VERSION` (`ClamAV x.y.z/<daily>/<ANSIC date>`); unparsable → stale. `/admin` shows date and age, warns at half the limit with the `refresh-scanner` hint; the vendor panel shows only the paused state ("the malware scanner's signatures are older than N days"), never the date.
- WHY: in image-only mode freshness equals refresh cadence; 7 or 14 days switches uploads off between releases for every operator without a cron (UI's own C1 dissent). 30 days is a real fail-closed bound matching monthly upgrades; 90 is the only relief; no skip flag.
- DISSENTING VIEW: UI (7 days — a week is stale for a "thorough" promise); PRIV, SRE (14). DA's "no gate" (B) was withdrawn in C.
- USER IMPACT: truthful paused copy.
- OPERATOR IMPACT: refresh monthly or uploads stop.
- TECHNICAL COST/RISK: the CI test database needs a dated `VERSION` reply — settled in A-192.
- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: fake `VERSION` 31 days old → upload POST refused (503 page), 0 rows, `/admin` "Signatures stale (31 days, limit 30)"; 29 days → accepted; `=3` → startup error naming the range.
- TEST PLAN: `clamd_test.go`: `TestClamdStaleRefuses`, `TestClamdVersionUnparsableIsStale`, `TestSignatureAgeRange`; `db-admin.spec.ts` "admin sees scanner signature date".

##### C3/D1 — drain scope on refusal

- DECISION: **MODIFY (drain every refusal on the upload route, never into memory)**. Order on `/listings/photo` and `/account/avatar`: method + Origin/Sec-Fetch-Site → `Content-Length` required (absent/chunked → 411 unread) and ≤ **cap + 4 MiB** (else 413 unread; reset accepted, documented) → session lookup under `takeLookupSlot` → sign-in, role vendor|admin, `write:` and `upload:` budgets, `uploadWork` slot, one-per-account lock → deadlines extended **only now** → first part `csrf` (≤128 B) → `id`, optional `description` → `photo` via `LimitReader(cap+1)`. Every refusal at any step copies the remainder to `io.Discard` up to the ceiling under the deadline in force (unextended 15 s before session+role; the route deadline after), then renders the HTML page via `httpError.Render` (as `adminHandleError` does), never `text/plain`. Beyond ceiling or deadline the reset stands.
- WHY: UI measured Firefox JS-off `NS_ERROR_NET_RESET` 4/4 without a drain and the 413 page 4/4 with one. The four seats objected to memory, slots and deadline extension for unauthenticated clients; `io.Discard`, no slot and the unextended 15 s satisfy all three, so an expired-session vendor gets "Sign in again" instead of a reset. A slow anonymous client already holds a connection 15 s today; the drain adds nothing the attacker does not pay byte-for-byte.
- DISSENTING VIEW: PRIV, SEC, DA, SRE — cut anonymous/expired/buyer early, drain only for a live vendor/admin session. Falsification: `TestUploadAnonDrainHoldsNoSlot` showing a slot, buffer or deadline extension → revert to the early cut.
- USER IMPACT: every refusal is a readable page stating the limit, listing form kept.
- OPERATOR IMPACT: none.
- TECHNICAL COST/RISK: the upload route must not use `http.MaxBytesReader` — when exceeded it calls the server's `requestTooLarge()` hook, which forces `Connection: close` and close-after-reply, so the response races the client's unsent bytes (the reset UI measured); use `io.LimitReader` with explicit accounting and drain from the same `r.Body`. PR #15's `busy()` sets `Connection: close` — on this route drain first. `admitWriter.Unwrap` (admission.go:153) lets `http.NewResponseController(w)` set deadlines; without a write deadline the 20 s `WriteTimeout` kills the 303 after a slow upload (SEC MISSED).
- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: raw client sends cap+1 MiB as a vendor → full 413 HTML naming the MB limit, no ECONNRESET; same as anonymous within 15 s → sign-in page; 2 MiB at 40 KiB/s → 303 arrives; memory and admission counters unchanged during an anonymous drain.
- TEST PLAN: `uploads_test.go`: `TestUploadRefusalReadsRestOfBody`, `TestUploadAnonDrainHoldsNoSlot`, `TestSlowUploadResponseArrives`, `TestUploadContentLengthOverCeilingUnread`; `db-listing-photos.spec.ts` "oversized photo shows the limit in Firefox without JS" (new `database-firefox` project limited to this spec).

##### C4 — signature-name handling

- DECISION: **ACCEPT (unanimous)**. Audit row fixed copy "Upload refused by the malware scanner"; uploader sees "Photo refused: the malware scanner flagged this file. Nothing was stored. If you think this is a mistake, contact the market staff. Reference: <8 hex>"; the reference and signature name appear together only in the one user-data-free log line (`upload outcome=refused reason=infected sig=<name> ref=<8 hex> kind=listing`); `/admin` shows 24 h counts by signature, unlinked to accounts; the reference is never written to `audit_events`.
- WHY: a false positive must not become a permanent signed record naming a vendor (DA MISSED); the name is not actionable for the uploader; the reference lets a vendor volunteer the link without the server storing it.
- DISSENTING VIEW: none after C.
- USER IMPACT: vendors get a reference to quote to staff.
- OPERATOR IMPACT: operators grep the log for it.
- TECHNICAL COST/RISK: nil.
- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: no `audit_events.text` contains a signature name or reference; the log line carries no handle, user id, listing id, filename or size.
- TEST PLAN: `TestRefusedUploadAuditHasNoSignatureName`, `TestUploadLogLineHasNoUserData`, `TestRefusedUploadLeavesNoRow`.

##### C5 — write deadline, session re-check, lock release, form nonce

- DECISION: **ACCEPT** (P0) the write deadline (`SetWriteDeadline(readDeadline+60 s)`), the commit-transaction re-check that the session row exists and the actor still holds the role, and `defer`-released one-per-account lock on body error/ctx cancel. **DEFER the form nonce to P2 (A-193)** with a trigger.
- WHY: three unanimous, cheap items. The nonce turns a bounded, visible, removable duplicate (4-per-listing cap) into a redirect, does not cover Back-then-reload (UI conceded), and costs a column, a unique index and an allow-list entry. Copy ("press Upload once and wait for the page to change") plus the lock release cover the common case.
- DISSENTING VIEW: PRIV, SEC, SRE, UI accepted the nonce (random 128-bit, deleted with the row); DA rejected it. Trigger: A-198 or live use shows repeated duplicates or "upload in progress" refusals.
- USER IMPACT: a double press yields one photo or a removable duplicate.
- OPERATOR IMPACT: none.
- TECHNICAL COST/RISK: nil now.
- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: a 2 MiB upload over 40 s ends in a complete 303; a session revoked during the scan commits no row; a client abort mid-body frees the account lock at once.
- TEST PLAN: `TestSlowUploadResponseArrives`, `TestRevokedSessionUploadDoesNotCommit` (revoke during a fake-clamd stall → 0 rows), `TestUploadLockReleasedOnClientAbort`.

##### C6/D9 — caps before the scan; ScanImage / ScanImageFuzzyHash

- DECISION: **ACCEPT (unanimous)**. Before the scan: byte cap (`Content-Length` + `io.LimitReader` accounting, C3), magic bytes (`FF D8 FF` / `89 50 4E 47 0D 0A 1A 0A`, else 415), a bounded header walk (≤64 KiB) plus `jpeg.DecodeConfig`/`png.DecodeConfig`: refuse w or h > 4096, w×h > 12 000 000, 4-component JPEG, or an estimated decode allocation > 64 MiB (w×h×bytes/px of the reported colour model; progressive JPEG (SOF2) counted at 12 B/px). Only bytes passing every cap go to clamd, once, unmodified; a pre-scan refusal stores nothing. `deploy/clamd.conf`: `ScanImage yes`, `ScanImageFuzzyHash no` (explicit, commented), `AlertExceedsMax yes`, `AlertBrokenMedia no`, `StreamMaxLength 9M`, `MaxFileSize`/`MaxScanSize` ≥ that, `ConcurrentDatabaseReload no`; FuzzyHash and AlertBrokenMedia revisited after A-197.
- WHY: the invariant is "nothing is stored or served without a clean scan"; a capped upload is not stored. The caps also shield clamd's own image decoder (SRE-E3). Fuzzy hashing is unmeasured for memory and false positives on phone JPEGs.
- DISSENTING VIEW: DA (leave FuzzyHash on, the header cap fronts clamd); UI (both off until a zero-false-positive corpus run).
- USER IMPACT: error copy names MB and pixel limits.
- OPERATOR IMPACT: none.
- TECHNICAL COST/RISK: the header walk also yields EXIF Orientation (DA-12).
- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: 4097×1 PNG refused before any IDAT read and before any clamd connection; progressive CMYK refused; a PNG carrying the CI test signature in a tEXt chunk passes caps and is refused by clamd (proves original bytes are scanned).
