- TEST PLAN: `images_test.go`: `TestDecodeConfigCapBeforeScan`, `TestRejectCMYK`, `TestProgressiveAllocationCap`; `tests/test_compose_scanner.py::test_clamd_conf_thorough_limits`.

##### D4 — takedown by hard delete; purge log vs restore warning

- DECISION: **MODIFY (hard delete + restore warning; no purge log, gate or stub)**. Staff takedown, owner removal and replace `DELETE` the row (full + thumb) in the action transaction; takedown records reason 10–500, one audit row (fixed copy + 8-char listing id + reason) to actor and owner, and `notifyOwner`. `scripts/restore.sh` prints `"<N> images restored from a backup taken <time>; photo and avatar removals made after that time are undone — check /moderator Recent photos and remove them again"`; UPGRADING §6 and the operator guide repeat it; "Recent photos" (A-190) is the re-check surface.
- WHY: SRE retracted the sha256 stub and gate (SEC: a deterministic re-encode makes the digest a confirmation oracle; PRIV: the restored DB cannot know later purges). PRIV's `backups/purges.log` needs a host-writable path the app lacks (container `read_only`, tmpfs only, no volume); a record kept in the DB is lost by the restore that undoes the purge.
- DISSENTING VIEW: PRIV — an id-only purge log outside the DB re-applied by `restore.sh`; revisit if the app ever gains an operator-writable volume. SEC's original hide/restore — declined (the vendor re-uploads after a mistaken takedown).
- USER IMPACT: vendors see "Photo removed by staff: <reason>" and a notification; buyers see the CSS illustration again.
- OPERATOR IMPACT: one line to read after a restore.
- TECHNICAL COST/RISK: nil.
- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: after takedown `/img/<id>-t|-f` → 404 identical to unknown; 0 rows; audit rows carry no image id, digest or name; `test-internal-db-restore.sh` and `test-backup-restore.sh` assert the printed line.
- TEST PLAN: `image_takedown_test.go`: `TestModeratorRemovesImageHardDelete`, `TestTakedownNotifiesOwnerWithReason`; the restore rehearsal scripts.

##### D7 — cache header

- DECISION: **ACCEPT `Cache-Control: private, max-age=86400, immutable`** on both image routes; pages stay `no-store`; no `ETag`/`Last-Modified`; `Content-Type: image/jpeg` constant; `nosniff`; `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`; `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`; `Content-Disposition: inline; filename="image.jpg"`; no `Set-Cookie`. Records disclose that a normal browser may keep viewed images in its disk cache for a day.
- WHY: JS-off browsers request every `<img>` eagerly (UI: 100/100); `no-store` refetches on every view over a 0.2–0.45 MB/s onion. With no validators a reload would refetch everything; `immutable` suppresses that and does not extend retention past the day DA and SRE objected to (their objection was to one year).
- DISSENTING VIEW: DA, SRE — omit `immutable`. Falsification: any browser shown to retain past `max-age` because of it → drop it. DA-E2 (keep `no-store`) closed here; A-198 measures the benefit.
- USER IMPACT: a second view costs no image bytes.
- OPERATOR IMPACT: none.
- TECHNICAL COST/RISK: nil.
- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: both image routes emit exactly the header set above, no `ETag`/`Last-Modified`, no `Set-Cookie`.
- TEST PLAN: `TestImageHeaders`.
- CORRECTION (chair, PR #28 review): max-age sets freshness, not retention; the records say so.

##### D8 — two image routes

- DECISION: **ACCEPT**. Public `GET/HEAD /img/<32 hex>-t|-f`: branches before any cookie/session code (beside `/static/` in `admitClass`), takes an `imageWork` slot (16; full → 503 + `Retry-After`, pages unaffected), one PK read joined to the public predicate (listing: `NOT p.archived`; avatar: owner `role IN ('vendor','admin') AND suspended_at IS NULL`). Authenticated `GET /desk/img/<id>-t|-f` under `pageWork` + `sessionLookupWork`: owner vendor or admin only (archived listings on listing-edit). Both 404 with a byte-identical body for unknown and hidden ids.
- WHY: keeps the public route free of session code and of DA-10's 26-lookups-per-catalog-view; unanimous.
- DISSENTING VIEW: DA — one route with a session lookup only when the predicate fails and a cookie is present; rejected: session code on the hot path and hidden ids probeable with a cookie.
- USER IMPACT: none visible.
- OPERATOR IMPACT: none.
- TECHNICAL COST/RISK: two registrations.
- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: a signed-in catalog view with 25 thumbnails does 1 session lookup; `imageWork` full → images 503, pages 200; archived/taken-down/demoted/suspended → anonymous 404, owner 200 on `/desk/img`.
- TEST PLAN: `image_routes_test.go`: `TestImageGetNoSessionLookup`, `TestImageFloodDoesNotRefusePages`, `TestImageHiddenAfterArchiveTakedownDemotionSuspension`, `TestImageUnknownAndHiddenIdentical`, `TestImageIDEntropy`.

##### D10 — caps

- DECISION: **ACCEPT** (consensus figures): body cap **4 MiB**; **4 photos per listing**; **1 avatar per user** (partial unique index); **50 MiB stored per uploader**; global `IMAGE_STORAGE_LIMIT_MB` default **2048**; `upload:<user>` **20 per 10 min** beside `write:` 100; one in-flight upload per account; `uploadWork` 2 nested in `userPostWork`; `imageDecodeWork` 1 nested in `uploadWork`; read deadline 90 s after admission. Output: full ≤1200 px long edge JPEG q82; thumbnail letterboxed to exactly 320×240 q70 (target ≤24 KiB, asserted ≤32 KiB on fixtures; A-198 may lower q); avatar 256×256 centre-cropped + 64×64; never upscale. Counts and sums computed in SQL in the insert transaction under the uploader's `users` row lock and `lockListing`; overflow → 409 page; global full → "Photo storage on this market is full; contact the operator". Memory budget documented beside `maxConns`: 128 MiB + 2×4 MiB + one decode ≤ 64 MiB estimate + outputs.
- WHY: figures converged in B/C; 4 MiB and 12 MP/4096 px cover a 4032×3024 phone JPEG.
- CORRECTION (chair, 2026-09-27, PR #22 review): the literal 12 000 000 px refused the 4032×3024 (12 192 768 px) and 4080×3060 phone JPEGs this WHY says the caps cover. The intent governs: the pixel cap is 4096×3072 (12 582 912 px); the 64 MiB decode estimate still bounds memory separately (4:2:0 baseline at the cap ≈ 36 MiB). The progressive estimate counts coefficient blocks at each component's own sampling (4:2:0 = 9 B/px, not 15).
- DISSENTING VIEW: SEC (3 MiB, 6 photos, 64 MiB, 30/hour); SRE (2 MiB or deadline); PRIV's 1200/320 adopted. A-196 may lower the pixel or allocation cap.
- USER IMPACT: panel shows "3 of 4 photos · 12 MB of 50 MB".
- OPERATOR IMPACT: `/admin` shows count, bytes vs limit and `pg_database_size`; guide gains "Disk sizing".
- TECHNICAL COST/RISK: nil.
- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: a 5th photo, a 2nd avatar, a per-uploader or global overflow → 409 page and 0 rows, including under concurrent requests; the 21st upload in 10 minutes → 429; a 2nd in-flight upload on one account → 429.
- TEST PLAN: `uploads_quota_test.go`: `TestPhotoPerListingCapConcurrent`, `TestVendorByteQuotaRace`, `TestGlobalStorageLimit`, `TestUploadBudget20Per10Min`, `TestOneUploadPerAccount`.

#### Owner decisions

Recorded 2026-09-27 (owner answers to the three questions):

1. **Moderator uploads**: the owner chose a **staff avatar shown beside the moderator's dispute decisions and on a small staff list** (2026-09-27). Consequences: avatar upload (`/account/avatar`, A-186/A-188) is open to vendors, administrators and moderators through the same re-encode + ClamAV path; the public avatar visibility predicate (A-187) becomes role in (vendor, admin, moderator) and not suspended; a new display slice in A-189 renders the staff avatar (alt="", 64 px thumbnail) beside each dispute decision on the order page and on a staff list on `/canary` (handle + avatar; coordinate with A-119's staff contact); the A-188 privacy warning applies to staff too (a staff photo links the moderator's identity across every decision). Moderators still take down vendor images through A-190.
   **Hiding staff from public view** (owner, 2026-09-27): (a) an administrator setting "Show a public staff list" (password-confirmed, audited old→new), **off by default**; while off, `/canary` shows no staff list and no staff avatar is served on the public image route; (b) each moderator and administrator has a per-account "Hide me from the public staff list" choice on `/account` (audited, default hidden), honoured even when the list is on; (c) a hidden staff member's avatar is served only through the authenticated route, beside their dispute decisions and only to that order's parties and staff (order pages are never public); (d) the public avatar predicate (A-187) adds: staff avatars only when the list is on and that account is not hidden, otherwise the same 404 as an unknown id. TESTS: `TestStaffListOffByDefault`, `TestHiddenStaffAvatarNotPublic`, `TestStaffAvatarVisibleToOrderParties`, `TestStaffListToggleAudited`; `preview-records`/canary spec asserts no staff list by default.
2. **Scan invariant**: accepted. "Nothing is stored or served without a clean `stream: OK` from clamd on the bytes received" satisfies "every upload scanned, no exceptions"; uploads refused by the byte, type and pixel caps before the scan are never stored.
3. **Tor-mode signature refresh**: accepted as recommended. Refresh on every `install.sh upgrade` plus a monthly `install.sh refresh-scanner`; staleness gate 30 days; offline operators use `docker save`/`load`; the freshclam-over-Tor path stays the A-195 trial.
4. **Implementation**: authorised, starting after PR #15 merges: A-110 first, then A-184–A-192.

### Iteration 11 ruling (chair, 2026-09-24) — A-150 hot P0 (ruled ahead of the identity cross-examination)

- **A-150 (ACCEPT, P0)**. WHY: a cheap, repeatable full-site outage reachable by any registered account (headline workflows unusable); fix is small and proven in the seat's scratch copy. ACCEPTANCE: `factorProvider.Enrolled` (and any other helper called from inside an action transaction) reads through the action's transaction (a row-querier interface, as `loadPGPAccount` already does), never `a.db`, while `c.Tx` is held; audit every action/loader for `a.db` use while holding `c.Tx` or another transaction and fix each (list them in the report); database errors from `Enrolled` return as errors (500), not 400 "Choose a verification method"; 16+ parallel POST /challenge on one pending cookie finish in < 1 s and a concurrent bystander GET in < 1 s. TEST: `TestChallengeBurstDoesNotExhaustPool` in `auth_gaps_test.go` (elapsed time and bystander latency asserted), failing first; existing identity suites under `-race`.

### Iteration 11 rulings (chair, 2026-09-24) — identity & account security sweep (security, backend, staff, buyer; Rounds A–C)

Evidence: seat reports in the session scratchpad (`iter11/{sec11,be11,staff11,buyer11}-{A,BC}.md`); Go DB probes by security and backend, live server + gpg by buyer; chair code-read of A-150. Order of work: A-150 → A-151 → A-152/A-157 → A-153 → A-154 → A-155/A-156/A-158.

- **A-151 (ACCEPT, P1)**. WHY: a cheap anonymous flood blocks every sign-in and every admin confirmation; the reorder is almost free. ACCEPTANCE: `authGate` takes a password slot only after handle format, CAPTCHA and limiter admit; `confirmPassword` takes the slot before charging `confirm:` and a 503 charges nothing; `/register` checks that the handle is free (and `lower(handle)` per A-87 if landed) before bcrypt and before any slot/budget. TEST: DB test — 32 concurrent unsolved-CAPTCHA POST /login while a correct-CAPTCHA real sign-in succeeds (repeat ≥10, all succeed); an admin `/admin/suspend` during the flood succeeds and the confirm budget is unchanged after a 503 path; taken-handle `/register` does not reach bcrypt (hook or timing-free counter).
- **A-152 (ACCEPT, P1; A-47 dissent re-opened and upheld)**. WHY: a stolen session must not be able to lock the owner out or redirect buyers' addresses, and the owner needs a signal when the thief has the password. DISSENT: backend would keep the password-always rule to accounts with a factor (A-47 posture) — overruled: the owner only types a password they know; the thief's factor otherwise locks them out and the notification is read too late (buyer, security). ACCEPTANCE: (1) password confirmation (via `confirmedTx`, plus factor when one exists) is required for `/totp/activate`, `/pgp/2fa` enable, PGP key change/removal, and replacing recovery codes, on every account including factor-less ones; (2) in the same transaction the owner gets one notification and one audit row for: TOTP on/off, recovery codes issued/replaced, recovery-code sign-in ("N remaining"), PGP key changed/removed/ownership proved, PGP sign-in on/off, and the first failed second-factor check per pending sign-in; (3) NEW "Signed in" rows name the method ("password and TOTP", "password and recovery code", …); existing audit rows are never rewritten (export byte-stable). TEST: DB tests per action — session without password → 400/403 and nothing changes; with password → change + exactly one notification + audit row; failed factor twice on one pending → one row; existing export fixture unchanged.
- **A-153 (MODIFY, P1)**. WHY: per-handle lockout is the accepted Tor trade-off (no per-IP signal), so it must cost the attacker a failed guess, be visible and have a documented break-glass. DISSENT: staff wanted the /admin refused-handles list in P1 — moved to A-159(b): the sole admin cannot see it while locked out (backend, security, buyer). ACCEPTANCE: `/register` never charges `auth:<handle>`; only FAILED passwords charge it (a correct sign-in does not); the 429 text says sign-in for this handle is paused for up to 10 minutes, someone else may have caused it, the password may be fine; one count-only log line per minute while the limiter refuses sign-ins (`sign-in limiter refusing handles=N`, never a handle); operator-guide documents who can trigger it, that the right password is refused, CAPTCHA-off makes it free, and restarting the app is the break-glass (then sign in at once). TEST: DB tests — 10 taken-handle registers then the right password succeeds; 11 correct sign-ins all succeed; 10 wrong then right → 429 with the new text; log line emitted with no handle (captured logger).
- **A-154 (ACCEPT, P1)**. WHY: the last recovery code must be enough to recover without staff; it grants no power beyond `/totp/disable` (password + recovery code). ACCEPTANCE: fix BE11-5 first — every path takes locks in one order (pending row → recovery row → user row; reset-admin CLI and password change/factor reset/`totpRegenerate` follow it); `/challenge` recovery form gains an unchecked "I lost my authenticator — turn two-step sign-in off" checkbox, honoured only with a recovery code, in the same `c.Tx`: turn TOTP off, clear `totp_last_step`, delete recovery codes/pending secret/reveal, audit "TOTP turned off at sign-in with a recovery code (N remaining)", notify (A-152), same session/pending handling as `/totp/disable`; suspended → whole tx rolls back; recovery sign-ins land on /totp showing "N recovery codes remain". Applies to admins. TEST: DB tests — checkbox + recovery code → TOTP off, codes gone, audit + notification; checkbox + TOTP code → ignored; suspended → TOTP still on; concurrent password change vs recovery sign-in → no deadlock (race test).
- **A-155 (MODIFY, P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: /account and /pgp show the primary key's and the encryption subkey's expiry; a daily sweep notifies the owner once when the encryption subkey expires within 30 days (text includes `gpg --quick-set-expire` renewal commands) and once when it has expired; the expired-key 409 on `/challenge/pgp` points to the A-119 lost-factor line; a same-fingerprint key update keeps ownership proof and PGP sign-in only when it is password-confirmed (A-152), every revocation signature in the stored key is still present, and the subkey `encryptTo` selects is the stored one or newer — otherwise verification is cleared with an explicit alert (never plain "Changes saved."). TEST: Go DB with generated keys — renewed expiry keeps sign-in; stripped subkey revocation → cleared + alert; near-expiry notification exactly once; pin in A-111's tests.
- **A-156 (ACCEPT, P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: host CLI `-set-role HANDLE ROLE` (A-28/A-137 harness, stdin-free): takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(782493)`, refuses to remove the last administrator, on removing admin deletes pending sign-ins then locks the user row then updates role and deletes sessions (suspend's order), archives listings when leaving vendor, writes an audit row "Role changed from X to Y by host command"; `confirmedTx` reads `role, suspended_at` from the row it already locks and refuses on mismatch; operator-guide "Administrators" subsection (add, remove, contain a compromised admin: demote → suspend on the web → review with A-143's handle filter). TEST: CLI tests (two concurrent demotions of the last two admins → exactly one succeeds); confirmed action racing a demotion → refused.
- **A-157 (MODIFY, P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: /account lists active sessions (count, start time to the minute, "this session"); "Sign out everywhere" also deletes pending sign-ins; the sessions panel says that only changing the password stops someone who knows it, and that a password change ends every other session and sign-in in progress. TEST: DB test — attacker pending sign-in then revoke → `/challenge` 401; page shows 2 sessions then 1.
- **A-158 (ACCEPT, P1)**. ACCEPTANCE: on a paid physical order the vendor key block says "This key was changed or re-verified on DATE, after this order was placed — confirm with the vendor before sending your address" when the key's verification/fingerprint change is newer than the order (existing columns; if none records the change time, record it in A-152's key-change path, new migration). TEST: DB test — key change after order → warning; before → none.
- **A-132 (MODIFY)**: CAPTCHA skipping, if ever, only by a GLOBAL trigger (process-wide failed sign-ins below a threshold and no password slot refused in the last minute), never on `/register` or while a handle is blocked, and only after A-151; accepted by BE11-E1's measurement. Stays open (P2) behind A-151.
- **A-87** amended: BUY11-5 case-insensitive sign-in moves to A-159(e); SEC11-3 severity SHOULD (lockout probe ran CAPTCHA-off).
- Declined: D-23 (UNIQUE lower index), D-24 (per-handle trigger; register key alone), D-25 (session-bound reveal), D-26 (web admin grant; 30-day refusal).

### Iteration 11 rulings (chair, 2026-09-24) — commerce, orders & disputes sweep (backend, staff, vendor, devil's advocate; Rounds A–C)

- **A-161 (ACCEPT, P1; implement together with A-122)**. WHY: a third party moves the whole received amount with one click next to text saying no funds move. ACCEPTANCE: one read-only helper `payoutBasis(tx, order)` returns the amount `enqueuePayout` would pay (non-locked deposits that are credited or at the provider's threshold; no provider → credited only), and `enqueuePayout`, the resolve radios and A-122's complete/cancel labels all use it; each resolve outcome states amount, recipient, difference from the price and the recipient's payout state ("no payout address: the payout waits for one", "account suspended: an administrator must check the address"); zero counted → "nothing counted yet; deposits confirming later go to the chosen party"; a required confirmation checkbox (missing → 400, dispute stays Open, no payout row); a hidden `amount_seen` compared inside `enqueuePayout` after its row locks (mismatch → 409 "The amount changed to X — review again", dispute stays Open; watcher calls pass no expectation); "moves no funds" is gone from every template, replaced by "Resolving queues one payout of the whole counted amount to one party; the payment watcher sends it automatically. There is no split."; the decision field carries A-80's plaintext warning (never quote an address, real name or tracking number). If the form is also rendered on /order, `/resolve` is the only staff form allowed there and only while disputed (pin it). TEST: `TestResolveStatesPayoutAmountAndNeedsConfirmation` (newPayEnv, 10× deposit), `TestResolveRefusesChangedAmount` (extra confirmed deposit between render and POST → 409, then 303), preview-orders desk assertions, template grep test for "moves no funds".

After shipping, a dispute sends the whole received amount to one party; there is no split."; implementation-status P3 states the all-or-nothing rule; complete and vendor-cancel use `payoutBasis` + `amount_seen` + required confirmation exactly as A-161 (one shared partial).

ACCEPTANCE: the resolve form (and desk entry) lists, per open dispute, messages sent since the dispute opened from each party to each staff member ("buyer → mod_a (2), vendor → none") from existing message metadata, never content; the resolve help says to consult those holders first; dispute-contacts copy tells parties that other staff can see which staff member they messaged and when, never what.
