### Owner directive (2026-09-29) — per-order escrow replaces the pooled wallet (WR-escrow)

The owner directed a payments redesign: instead of pooling coins in one market wallet (custodial, exposed to an exit scam or a single compromise), each order gets its own escrow that the buyer pays within a set time. Digital (auto-delivery) orders resolve automatically on funding with the delivered note; physical orders keep funds in escrow until shipped/confirmed; buyer and vendor talk on the order page; in a dispute an administrator decides refund to the buyer or release to the vendor. The network fee is deducted from the escrowed amount (refund included); the market's fee from a fulfilled order goes to an administrator-configured address per coin. Owner answers to the chair's questions (2026-09-29): **(1) Bitcoin keys: buyer, vendor and market** — true non-custodial 2-of-3 (buyer and vendor register a key/xpub from their own wallet; the market holds only the arbitrator key; signing by pasting PSBTs into HTML forms, no JavaScript); **(2) Monero: per-order wallets now** (custodial per order, compartmentalised, no pool), 2-of-3 multisig evaluated later; **(3) pooled mode: keep until escrow ships, then remove** — safety fixes only in the pooled system, no new pooled features, removal with an upgrade path for open orders once escrow is verified end to end on regtest/testnet; **(4) auto-delivery: the market co-signs the vendor's claim** — on funding the content is delivered and the order completes; the vendor claims whenever they like by signing a PSBT the server co-signs automatically with the market key (kept hot for this; the server alone still cannot move funds). OPEN QUESTION (chair, for the council and then the owner): the owner wrote that physical-order funds stay in escrow "until the product is shipped and marked as shipped by the user" — either the vendor marks it shipped and the market co-signs the release (vendor paid at shipping; less buyer protection), or the buyer confirms receipt and signs the release (with a timeout and the dispute path as backstops); the council recommends, the owner decides. Process: a war-room design council (WR-escrow, Rounds 0–D with real regtest/stagenet-fork feasibility spikes) rules the phased design before any implementation; invariants 2 and 4 in repo-map.md updated.


### WR-escrow Round D ruling (chair, 2026-09-29) — architect, payments, security, privacy, buyer/vendor; Rounds 0, A, B+C

Evidence: Round 0 architecture pack and real-chain spikes, five Round A reports and five Round B+C replies (chair scratchpad `wr-escrow/`), each seat's own probes on Bitcoin Core 31.1 regtest and Monero 0.18.5.1 regtest (real local chain, NOT retained under A-103). Proven on regtest this round: a comma in a key field makes a 2-of-4 one party spends alone (SEC); a market signature made with SIGHASH_NONE lets the vendor redirect a refund and passes `testmempoolaccept` (SEC); a later market-signed outcome RBF-replaces an earlier broadcast one (PAYE P1); the watch-only wallet builds a PSBT over a spent outpoint (P2); a forged entry under the market pubkey merged into the stored copy blocks signing (SEC); `sendrawtransaction` of the same tx is idempotent (P3); a reorg + double-spend drops the txid from `listreceivedbyaddress` (P5); a Monero `do_not_relay` build does not lock outputs and a rebuilt sweep pays a late deposit to the vendor (P6); Monero per-order accounts: 300 accounts polled in one refresh + `get_transfers{all_accounts}` (0.3 s) and a spend beyond one account's balance refused with -17 (P7); the arbiter descriptor wallet signs at index 5000/19000 after `keypoolrefill` (ARCH, PAYE, SEC, PRIV); `rpcwhitelist` refuses key export while allowing signing (SEC); a practice PSBT is checkable with throwaway keys and never broadcastable (BUY). NOT proven: Sparrow, Electrum and hardware-wallet export/import/signing (E1); real-chain Monero account scan cost at 10k accounts (PAYE-E2); `keypoolrefill` under `rpcwhitelist` and on an encrypted wallet.

**Ruled design (binding for implementers; every item below is an acceptance criterion of the phase that builds it):**
- **ES-1 Settlement model (C1 + ARCH).** No new order states. Settlement is its own table with CAS states (building → awaiting_signature → party_signed → signed → broadcast → confirmed | external | failed) and order_events notes. The database refuses BOTH a second outcome per order (`UNIQUE(order_id) WHERE kind IN ('release','refund')`; `stray_refund` is its own kind) AND a second claim of any outpoint (`payments.settlement_id` claimed by CAS; `payments` stays the per-UTXO ledger — PAYE-8's separate table declined). Both terminal-transition call sites dispatch through one `settleTerminal` (ARCH-1); an escrow order never gets a `payouts` row and the send claim excludes escrow orders. Transitions stay RPC-free; the watcher builds, co-signs and broadcasts (ARCH-7).
- **ES-2 Signing pipeline (C2 + SEC-2 + PAYE-2/3).** The server builds every settlement itself (explicit confirmed, unspent inputs checked with `gettxout`; no wallet coin selection; outputs from the frozen plan; explicit fee rate; absolute and rate fee caps). A pasted PSBT is accepted only if its unsigned txid equals the stored one, it carries a signature on every input from the expected party's derived key (never the market key; an entry under the market key is refused), and every signature ends in 0x01; unknown/global/proprietary fields are ignored and the pasted blob is never stored or re-served. The market signs its pristine stored copy with `"ALL"` hard-coded, only in a terminal order state, only from the watcher; then `combinepsbt([market_signed, pasted])` market copy first; the final tx is re-checked (txid, outputs, fee ≤ cap, every witness signature 0x01), `testmempoolaccept` with explicit `maxfeerate`, persisted raw before the first broadcast, and only that stored tx is ever rebroadcast (Bitcoin "txid returned" and -27 are success). Never rebuild a settlement that has a signed or broadcast attempt. Only the server broadcasts; a market-signed PSBT is never shown to anyone (BUY-13 retracted); after broadcast the raw tx may be shown. Fee bumps (same recipients, only the claimant's output shrinks) are Phase 2; Phase 1 relies on recipient CPFP.
- **ES-3 Keys (C3 + C4 + K2 + K4).** One key expression per party, parsed strictly in Go before any RPC (`[fp/path]tpub…` with origin; refuse commas, parentheses, `#`, whitespace, any `prv`, WIF or word lists — a pasted private key gets "this key is exposed, replace it"). BIP48 paths accepted plainly; other paths only with a "wallet used only for this market" acknowledgement (Core exports only 44/49/84/86h accounts); SLIP-132 single-sig prefixes refused. Registration and change need `confirmedTx` step-up, notify, and never affect existing escrows. The account xpub is sealed with the keyRoot sealer; cross-user reuse is refused by `UNIQUE(HMAC(xpub))`. Per order the server expands child keys statelessly (`createpsbt` + `utxoupdatepsbt [xpub descriptor]`), stores only the three bare child pubkeys (`escrow_pubkeys(pubkey PRIMARY KEY)`) plus each party's own origin, imports only a bare-key `wsh(sortedmulti(2,…))` into the watch-only wallet, and checks the composed witness script (3 distinct compressed keys, exactly one derived from the arbiter fingerprint read from Core) before showing the address. A fresh market child index per escrow from a database counter; `escrows.address` UNIQUE. The arbiter key lives in a dedicated Core descriptor wallet; the app's RPC user runs under `rpcwhitelist` (escrow methods, `keypoolrefill`, no key export, dump or backup); the watcher refills the keypool ahead of the index high-water mark; startup pins the arbiter fingerprint and disables escrow on a mismatch. No market xpub or vendor account xpub is ever published (fingerprint pin only — SEC-5's canary xpub and PGP-published vendor xpub declined as privacy harms). Each party sees and downloads only its own origin (others bare); per-party PSBT copies via `utxoupdatepsbt`. **E1 result and OWNER DECISION 2026-09-29 ("Core now, Electrum later"):** Bitcoin Core signs this shape; Electrum 4.5.8/4.8.2 cannot (it signs only a 2-of-3 wallet built from three per-order Vpubs). Phase 1 supports Core signing only; the schema stores each party's full derivation suffix and an optional per-order xpub (no bare `/0/i` index column) so an Electrum/Sparrow shape can follow without a corrective migration (A-255).
- **ES-4 Who signs.** A settlement's outputs are frozen and pinned; the server accepts the one signature from EITHER escrow party (whoever signs first) and then co-signs; if both parties sign, the market does not sign. Digital orders: completion on funding (escrow-only system edge delivered→completed), then the vendor claims. Refunds, dispute outcomes and stray refunds take the same path. Dispute decisions on escrow orders are **administrator-only with `confirmedTx` step-up** (SEC-8; the owner's directive says "administrator"; ARCH retracted ARCH-4; a `roleAdmin` edge behind the escrow guard map) and cannot change once a market signature exists.
- **ES-5 Destinations and fees (C8 + PRIV-2 + PAYE-6).** The buyer's refund address is required and pinned at checkout (refused if the market already used it as an escrow, settlement or fee destination); the vendor's destination is taken when the settlement is built (fresh, single-use), never at signing. Fee bps and the fee destination are snapshotted at checkout; the network fee comes from the claimant's output only; the market-fee output is never reduced; outputs below dust/floor fold into the claimant's output with an order note; one floor per coin, no parallel copy (ARCH-14). The fee destination setting accepts a plain address or a watch-only descriptor (a fresh address per settlement via `deriveaddresses` on a counter); **OWNER DECISION 2026-09-29: descriptor on mainnet** — on a mainnet market the fee destination must be a watch-only descriptor (fresh fee address per settlement, index frozen in the settlement); a single fixed address is allowed on test networks with a warning.
- **ES-6 Restore and reconciliation (C7 + PAYE-4).** The restore gate also pauses market co-signing, Monero relay and new builds; watching continues. Reconciliation reads the chain (outpoints spent by an unknown txid → `external`, flagged; escrows in the wallet but not the database → orphan rows for staff; signed attempts rebroadcast only after the operator confirms).
- **ES-7 Monero (C6 + K1 + K8).** Escrow wallets run on their own monero-wallet-rpc, driven only by the watcher under its advisory lock; checkout claims a pre-created slot by database CAS and makes no RPC. Every spend names the escrow's `account_index` (never 0, never absent) after checking the open wallet's primary address; settlement is one transaction with `subtract_fee_from_outputs`, stored and relayed with `relay_tx`, never rebuilt. Wallet passwords come from a dedicated `MONERO_WALLET_SECRET` (never keyRoot or SETUP_TOKEN — rotating those must never lock funds); each wallet's seed is PGP-encrypted to the operator key and stored in the database at creation. Custody copy everywhere: "held by this market in a wallet used only for this order; the market can move these coins" — never "escrow the market cannot spend". **OWNER DECISION 2026-09-29: accounts in monthly wallets** — one escrow wallet per month holding one account per order (account 0 never used and kept empty; every spend names the order's account), each monthly wallet destroyed (file and sealed seed) 90 days after its last order closes; late deposits within that window refunded automatically; per-order wallet files (and ARCH-9 scheduling, SEC-4's serializer as a BLOCKER) are not built. A later Monero multisig evaluation would need per-order wallet files again.
- **ES-8 Retention and late funds (K9).** Bitcoin escrows stay imported (bare keys) and are checked daily plus on demand by a buyer "Recover coins at this escrow" action (buyer + market, auto co-sign) — no age limit. Monero: late deposits within the retention window are refunded to the pinned address automatically; after it the page says "Monero sent to this order's address after <date> cannot be recovered".
- **ES-9 Key activation (K6).** A key is inactive until its owner signs a practice PSBT: a reserved child index never used for escrows, throwaway server keys (never the market key), a fabricated prevout checked absent with `gettxout` (never broadcastable); offered only while the key has no escrows; nothing stored but "active at <time>". Required before any escrow is created, from Phase 1.
- **ES-10 Honest copy and privacy (SEC-5 revised, PRIV-6/7/10, BUY-10).** The Bitcoin claim is qualified ("the market cannot spend your escrow without you or the other party, if the key shown is yours; checking the descriptor in your wallet is what detects a substituted key"); the order page states the counterparty can trace funding and settlement on chain; audit and order-event text is fixed (no keys, fingerprints, destinations, PSBTs); RPC errors are mapped to fixed classes before logging; `/canary#records` gains the escrow records; docs per A-237's documentation criterion.
- **ES-11 Physical orders (OPEN QUESTION → owner).** Council 5/5 (SEC-14's buyer-signature variant withdrawn): release when the buyer clicks "Confirm receipt" (password step-up; authorises the market co-sign of the vendor's claim) or when a per-order auto-finalize deadline after "shipped" passes with no dispute (buyer notified at shipped and 72 h before; a dispute stops the clock); never release at "shipped"; a buyer may cancel and be refunded (buyer + market) if the vendor has not shipped within the listing's dispatch window plus grace. **OWNER DECISION 2026-09-29 (against the council's recommendation): the buyer must sign the release**, with a timeout — a physical order releases when the buyer signs the release PSBT in their own wallet together with the vendor (buyer + vendor; the market does not sign), or, if the buyer has not signed and no dispute is open, when the per-order auto-release deadline passes (then vendor + market, the market co-signing automatically); **deadline: 14 days after "shipped" by default, administrator-adjustable within bounds (7–30 days), snapshotted per order** (buyer notified at shipped and 72 h before; a dispute stops the clock). Never release at "shipped". Consequences for Phase 2: the buyer's release page is a PSBT signing page like the vendor's (same ES-2 checks, expected-outputs table, paste-keeping errors); ES-9 practice-sign matters more (a buyer who cannot sign falls back to the deadline, not to a stuck order); the vendor-cancel/no-ship refund path is unchanged.

**Phases (each its own PR, own migrations, CI green, docs claim only the verified tier):**
- **Phase 0 (no escrow behaviour):** A-218 (partial unique index) plus the `settleTerminal` dispatcher and escrow guard map with `Order.Escrow` always false, and step-up on `/resolve` (A-32). Gate: DB integration + upgrade test.
- **E1 before any Phase 1 migration merges (migrations are append-only):** scripted Electrum offline signing on regtest of a served per-party PSBT (own origin, cosigners bare) for a fixed per-order child key, plus Core; Sparrow manual later. A failure switches ES-3 to per-order child xpubs (PRIV-3 revised) before any schema lands.
- **Phase 1 (regtest, `ESCROW_BTC=on`, off by default):** Bitcoin escrow for digital auto-delivery listings: ES-1–ES-6, ES-8 (BTC), ES-9, ES-10; refund on expiry/underpay/vendor cancel, admin dispute, stray refund; static fee address allowed. Gate: Go DB tests with a fake escrow provider for every edge; httptest JSON-RPC adapter tests (exact params, sighash pinned, whitelist); `scripts/test-escrow-regtest.sh` legs (release, refund, dispute, comma injection refused, NONE paste refused, forged market slot refused, conflicting outcome refused, spent-outpoint build refused, restart mid-send rebroadcasts the same tx, reorg + double-spend flagged, high index via keypoolrefill, restore gate + reconciliation, two escrows never mixed, fee cap) with logs; Playwright no-JS paste journey at 320 px against the simulated RPC fixture extended with PSBT methods.
- **Phase 2:** physical orders per the owner's ES-11 answer (buyer signs the release; 14-day administrator-adjustable deadline; no-ship cancel); same-recipient fee bumps; browser DB journey of buyer signing, deadline release and dispute.
- **Phase 3:** Monero per the owner's ES-7 answer (per-order accounts in monthly wallets); Monero-regtest legs (ring 16, never the HF-v1 fork for fees); backup/restore extended; PAYE-E2 measured.
- **Phase 4:** public test networks (testnet4/signet, stagenet) end to end, retained; E1 matrix incl. Sparrow; fee descriptor required on mainnet (owner's ES-5 answer); private broadcast in Tor mode (PRIV-15); cutover release (new orders escrow-only), then the pooled-removal release with a drain gate (ARCH-10). Mainnet stays an operator opt-in, never used in development or tests.

Pooled-system items that become moot at removal (keep open, safety fixes only until then): A-142, A-56, A-33/A-22, A-105; carried into escrow form: A-17 (reorg script), A-15, A-76, A-55, A-84 (PAYE-13).

### WR-escrow Phase 2 ruling (chair, 2026-10-03) — payments seat (Opus), buyer/vendor journey seat (Sonnet); lean pass on ES-11

Scope A-247 (physical orders, owner decision ES-11) plus A-366, A-371 to A-378. Regtest only, behind ESCROW_BTC (off by default); no browser JavaScript; migrations 074–078 untouched, new ones numbered in merge order from 079. Built on the local branch escrow/phase2 (from main cd253c4) and shipped as one Phase 2 PR.

Owner decisions 2026-10-03 (AskUserQuestion, recommended options): dispatch window per listing 1–30 days, default 3, buyer may cancel 48 h after the window (counted from payment confirmation); a dispute filed after release_at is refused (the release is due); after a restore, any passed release deadline becomes now + 72 h with a buyer notice; A-366 margin 50 per restore.

- **P2-R1 Release at shipped.** For an escrow order, "Mark shipped" takes a fresh vendor claim address and a password step-up and, in one transaction, moves paid → shipped, inserts the release settlement (kind release, destination) and snapshots release_at = now + the escrow's release_days. The watcher builds and tracks a shipped physical release (settlementBuildable); settlementFits stays terminal-only and gates every market signature.
- **P2-R2 Buyer signs; the market never signs before the outcome.** The buyer and the vendor sign through the existing sign action. A new parties-only finalize (both signatures, no arbiter call) stores the final tx with cosigned 'parties' and moves shipped → completed (system, escrowOnly edge) in the same transaction under the order row lock, rechecking shipped with no dispute. A failing signature on a shipped order is discarded and never falls back to the market (A-372); the buyer's pooled Complete is refused on escrow orders (A-373). A new trigger refuses a stored raw tx with cosigned 'market' unless the order state fits the settlement kind (database backstop for A-372).
- **P2-R3 Deadline.** escrow_release_days setting: default 14, 7–30, administrator with confirmedTx, audited old → new; snapshotted per escrow (release_days, release_at; frozen by trigger). Watcher step releaseDue (no RPC; skipped while the restore gate is set): shipped, no dispute, release_at <= now → system transition to completed ("auto-release deadline passed"); the existing co-sign step then co-signs the vendor's stored signature. Reminder at release_at − 72 h exactly once (CAS on reminded_at). A dispute filed after release_at is refused.
- **P2-R4 Disputes stop the clock for good.** Disputed only goes to resolved, so nothing resumes. Decision for the buyer: in the resolve transaction supersede the pre-built release if it has no raw_tx, free its payments, insert the refund (A-371); if the release already has a final tx, refuse with 409 naming why. Decision for the vendor: reuse the existing release (fix the note that asks for a new claim). Copy says "the deadline no longer applies", never "paused".
- **P2-R5 No-ship cancel.** products.dispatch_days (1–30, default 3); escrows snapshot dispatch_days and ship_by = paid time + dispatch_days + 48 h (set in the paid transition). New edge paid → cancelled for the buyer, escrow only, refused before ship_by with the date stated; ship and cancel race on the row lock, exactly one wins, and the loser gets a specific message.
- **P2-R6 Top-ups while paid or shipped (A-374).** A watcher step marks escrow_left_out on any deposit no settlement covers once a release or refund is past building, with an order note and notices to the buyer and staff; the stray refund returns it after close. Shipped releases are tracked like terminal ones (A-376).
- **P2-R7 Same-recipient fee bumps.** Child tables settlement_bumps and settlement_bump_signatures with their own freeze and forward-only triggers, one open bump per settlement; the parent row is never changed. Go checks at build and finalize: identical inputs and output scripts and order, only the claimant's output smaller, market fee and buyer return unchanged, BIP125 increment, within the fee caps, claimant output at or above the floor, parent broadcast and unconfirmed. The claimant must sign (vendor for a release, buyer for a refund or stray refund), then the market or the other party; never the non-claimant plus the market. The watcher's own-txid set is the parent plus every stored bump: a conflict inside it is not external (A-375), and only the highest-fee stored attempt is re-sent. scripts/restore.sh holds bump rows with a raw tx.
- **P2-R8 Physical checkout.** escrowEligible extends to physical listings with a dispatch window; the escrow checkout checks userCanReceiveEncrypted(vendor) before any RPC (A-83) and the offer gives the reason; checkout snapshots release_days and dispatch_days. The shipping address flow is unchanged (PGP to the vendor, never stored).
- **P2-R9 Journey and notices (A-377, A-378).** Every shipped, reminder, release and dispute notice carries the order short ID and the deadline in absolute UTC; /orders shows a banner for escrow orders with under 72 h left. Order page: a "who acts next" line, the absolute deadline with a load-time countdown, the release signing section reusing Phase 1's expected-outputs table and paste form, "If you cannot sign, you need do nothing… open a dispute first if the goods did not arrive", signing equals confirming receipt, the Mark shipped warning, the no-ship cancel note before and after the window, the dispute line, and the restore and unavailable states. Corrections: escrow dispute help names an administrator; "Either you or the other party can sign" and the "market co-signs it on its next pass" text are false for a physical release before the deadline.
- **P2-R10 A-366.** Exact layer: escrow_reserved_keys filled by reconcileOrphans from getaddressinfo on each orphan (UNVERIFIED that Core 31.1 returns pubkeys for an imported wsh(sortedmulti) — a regtest leg must confirm before relying on it); buildUnwatchedEscrow skips any child key in it or in escrow_pubkeys; restore.sh sets escrow_sweep_required and checkout answers 503 until a full orphan sweep has run. Margin layer: restore.sh writes escrow_index_floors = max + 50 per account and moves escrow_market_index by 50; escrowParties reads the floor; the order page advises keypoolrefill once a party's next index passes 900. Restore also moves passed release deadlines to now + 72 h with a buyer notice (release_recheck).

**Slices (waves; QA per slice; model per owner direction 2026-10-03):**
- **P2-a (Sonnet; wave 1):** migration 079 (products.dispatch_days; escrows release_days, dispatch_days, ship_by, release_at, reminded_at, release_recheck; freeze trigger), the release-days setting and /admin form, the listing dispatch field, physical escrow checkout with the A-83 check and snapshots. Tests TestEscrowPhysicalCheckout, TestEscrowCheckoutVendorKeyUnusable, TestEscrowReleaseDaysSetting, a 079 trigger test. Mutants: drop A-83; live setting instead of snapshot; bounds 6/31; drop the freeze trigger.
- **P2-d (Opus; wave 1, parallel):** fee bumps (P2-R7, A-375): migration 080, escrow_bump.go, track functions in escrow_cosign.go, restore.sh, the settlement partial. Tests TestEscrowBumpClaimantOnly, TestEscrowBumpConfirmsNotExternal, TestEscrowBumpShapeRefusals; regtest TestEscrowRegtestBump. Mutants: non-claimant accepted; market fee may shrink; conflict check external-only; parent re-sent after a bump.
- **P2-b (Opus; wave 2):** P2-R1, R2, R4, R6 with A-371 to A-374 and A-376; migration 081 (market-signature guard). Tests TestEscrowPhysicalBothSign, TestEscrowShippedNeverMarketSigns, TestEscrowDisputeSupersedesRelease, TestEscrowTopUpWhileShipped, TestEscrowMigration081Guard; regtest TestEscrowRegtestPhysical (arbiter walletprocesspsbt count 0); Playwright buyer signing. Mutants: the fallback at escrow_cosign.go:310 reachable; settlementFits back at the build gate; no supersede; complete on paste instead of a verified final tx; no left-out step.
- **P2-c (Opus; wave 3):** P2-R3, R5, the restore recheck; notices and the /orders banner (A-377). Tests TestEscrowDeadlineRelease, TestEscrowDeadlineDisputeRace, TestEscrowDeadlineGatePaused, TestEscrowReminderOnce, TestEscrowNoShipCancel, TestEscrowLateDisputeRefused, TestEscrowRestoreExtendsDeadline; regtest deadline leg; Playwright deadline release, dispute and no-ship cancel. Mutants: no dispute check; < for <=; no gate check; no reminder CAS; no ship_by check.
- **P2-e (Opus; wave 4):** P2-R10 (A-366); migration 083 (082 went to P2-d-fix). Tests TestEscrowRestoreSkipsReservedKeys, an httptest getaddressinfo pubkeys test, a regtest leg proving an empty pubkey intersection. Mutants: address-only compare; no sweep gate; margin 0.
- **P2-f (Sonnet; after P2-e):** docs (escrow.md Phase 2 section and evidence tiers, operator guide, implementation status, acceptance, UPGRADING for 079–082, runbook) and the full no-JS journey spec tests/e2e/escrow-physical-journey.spec.ts with the journey seat's ten checks.

Gate for the Phase 2 PR: every commit builds and vets; race DB suite; Python; govulncheck; Chromium preview and DB; escrow journeys; scripts/test-escrow-regtest.sh on Core 31.1 with the new legs; test-upgrade.sh through the last new migration; test-backup-restore.sh; gitleaks. Not covered and not claimed: public test networks, mainnet, wallets other than Bitcoin Core.

### WR-escrow Phase 3 ruling (chair, 2026-10-03) — payments seat (Opus); lean pass on ES-7

Scope A-248 (Monero, owner decision ES-7: one escrow wallet per month, one account per order). Monero escrow runs on a local fakechain only (monerod --regtest, Monero's regtest, the counterpart of Phase 1's Bitcoin regtest under invariant 2), behind ESCROW_XMR (off by default); no browser JavaScript; migrations 074–083 untouched, new ones numbered in merge order from 084. Built on the local branch escrow/phase3 (from main 7f29e4f) and shipped as one Phase 3 PR. The owner directed best code quality with models matched to the work (2026-10-03): Opus for every slice and QA pass that touches money movement, wallet keys or seeds; Sonnet for docs.

Chair-verified against the code before ruling: the pooled adapter is hard-wired to account 0 and an empty wallet password and refuses fakechain (payments_monero.go get_address, open_wallet, network check), so escrow gets its own adapter; settlements cannot hold Monero (address CHECK 14–90 characters, a required psbt, final-tx triggers keyed on signatures, 075/076/078), so Monero settlements get a sibling table; escrows is generic apart from market_index, and every pay-twice guard (payouts_not_escrow, the send claim, Order.Escrow, actions_orders, orders_load) keys on it, so Monero escrows share it (a missed coin filter in Bitcoin code fails closed, a missed guard on a sibling table would pay twice); the network-class startup check reads payment_addresses.provider only, and escrow addresses never enter payment_addresses.

- **P3-R1 Schema (084).** escrows gains coin ('BTC' default, 'XMR'), equal to orders.currency (trigger); market_index becomes nullable with CHECK coin='BTC' iff market_index IS NOT NULL; xmr_slot_id. New tables: xmr_escrow_wallets (name escrow-YYYYMM-NN UNIQUE, month, primary_address UNIQUE, restore_height, seed_sealed, seed_key_fpr, sealed_at, state creating, sealed, active, closing, retained, destroyed, missing, orphan; CHECK active or closing implies a seal; last_closed_at, destroy_after, destroyed_at); xmr_escrow_slots (wallet_id, account_index >= 1, address UNIQUE, state free, claimed, retired, order_id UNIQUE; UNIQUE wallet_id, account_index); xmr_settlements (kind release, refund, stray_refund; destination exactly 95 characters (a standard address or subaddress; integrated addresses are refused, P3-R2); tx_hash, tx_metadata, tx_blob, fee, input_total, key_images; states requested, built, relayed, confirmed, external, failed, superseded) and xmr_settlement_outputs (claimant, market_fee, buyer_return). Triggers: the stored tx is frozen once tx_hash is set; forward-only states (077 pattern, one confirmed to relayed step for a reorg); tx_hash stored only when escrow_market_may_sign(kind, order_id) (081) allows it; one live release or refund per order; at most one built-not-relayed row per order; release destinations unique; settlements refuses an XMR escrow and xmr_settlements a BTC one. payments stays the ledger (ES-1): xmr_settlement_id; Monero escrow rows aggregate per transaction and account with idx = 2^62 + wallet_id·2^32 + account_index (never a pooled minor index; every account's minor index is 0), and an insert conflict that names another order is flagged to staff, never merged. Every Bitcoin escrow query that could read a Monero row filters coin='BTC'.
- **P3-R2 Network.** The escrow daemon must report nettype fakechain, hard fork version >= 16 and height below 1,000,000; the escrow wallet-rpc's own height after refresh must be below 1,000,000 and within 10 blocks of the daemon's (a wallet-rpc attached to another daemon is refused). Refused at startup when the market runs mainnet (a.live, MONERO_NETWORK=mainnet or BITCOIN_CHAIN=main) and when ESCROW_XMR_WALLET_RPC_URL equals MONERO_WALLET_RPC_URL. Mainnet-format (4, 8) refund and claim addresses are accepted only on this fakechain-only escrow path; the pooled adapter is unchanged and still refuses fakechain. Addresses are parsed offline in Go (base58 blocks and the Keccak checksum via golang.org/x/crypto/sha3, already a dependency); integrated addresses, any escrow or wallet primary address and the fee address are refused.
- **P3-R3 Wallets and slots (watcher only).** The Monero escrow watcher has its own advisory lock and loop; monero-wallet-rpc holds one wallet open at a time, so each pass cycles open_wallet, refresh, work, store with a longer timeout than rpcTimeout. Creation: insert creating; create_wallet with the derived password; query_key mnemonic sealed to the pinned seed key; store the seal and restore height (sealed); next month's wallet is created at least 7 days before the month so a daily backup holds its seal before it takes an order; it becomes active at the month's start, and the old one becomes closing (its free slots retired). The watcher keeps ESCROW_XMR_SLOTS (default 20, 5–200) free slots on the active wallet by create_account; account 0 is never a slot, never spent, and a balance there alerts staff. Checkout makes no RPC: it claims a free slot of an active wallet by compare-and-set (FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED) and inserts the escrow in the same transaction; no free slot answers 503 and the order stays a draft.
- **P3-R4 Secrets and seeds.** Wallet password = hex HMAC-SHA256(MONERO_WALLET_SECRET, "opsecmkt xmr escrow wallet" NUL name); the secret is at least 32 bytes and differs from SETUP_TOKEN and APP_SECRET_KEY, or startup is refused; a verifier is pinned in settings and a mismatch disables Monero escrow with one fixed line (no wallet created). The seed is sealed to ESCROW_XMR_SEED_KEY, an armored OpenPGP public key file with an encryption-capable key, a subkey or the primary key (ProtonMail go-crypto, already a dependency), never the canary key (that can be changed from /admin); its fingerprint is pinned on first use; a changed key stops wallet creation until the operator re-pins it with the documented SQL; seeds are never re-sealed automatically. The runbook has an offline check: decrypt one seed, restore it, compare the primary address.
- **P3-R5 Funding.** Deposits come from get_transfers with all_accounts; a deposit counts at confirmations >= max(PAYMENT_CONFIRMATIONS_XMR, 10) with unlock_time 0 and no double_spend_seen (the pooled checks); a locked deposit is frozen and flagged. A digital order completes on funding through the existing escrow-only system edge.
- **P3-R6 Settlement (watcher only, ES-7).** Gate check; get_address account 0 must equal the stored primary address and account N address 0 the escrow address; incoming_transfers for account N; freeze every output the settlement does not claim and thaw the claimed ones; the unlocked unfrozen total must equal the claimed total, otherwise wait or flag; transfer with account_index N (never 0 or absent), destinations claimant, market fee, buyer return, subtract_fee_from_outputs on the claimant only, do_not_relay, get_tx_metadata, get_tx_hex; spent_key_images must equal the claimed outputs' key images exactly and amounts_by_dest the plan (claimant = plan minus fee, market fee exact); the fee must not exceed ESCROW_XMR_MAX_FEE (piconero; default sized for fakechain, whose young chain charges about 0.002 XMR for two outputs, chair probe 2026-10-03) and the claimant output must stay at or above the Monero release floor (A-232), otherwise the build is discarded unrelayed, the settlement waits and staff are told; the tx is stored (built) before relay_tx with the stored metadata. Ambiguous relay or restart: look the tx up in the daemon and the wallet; absent, relay the same stored metadata again (same key images, so it cannot pay twice). Definite refusal: key images spent by another tx records external and alerts staff; otherwise failed and staff are alerted. Never rebuilt automatically. OWNER DECISION 2026-10-03 (O-1, A-401): an administrator may re-request a failed settlement with a password step-up, audited, only when the stored tx is in neither the daemon's pool nor the chain and every stored key image is unspent; the new build must spend exactly the stored key images to the stored plan (destinations and amounts; only the fee and the claimant's output may change by the fee), the old row becomes superseded in the same transaction, and the same checks then apply (stored before relay). A confirmed settlement that a reorg removes goes back to relayed and is relayed again; followed to 100 confirmations.
- **P3-R7 Releases.** Digital: the vendor claims with a fresh Monero address and a password step-up, as escrowClaimAction does; built after the 10-block unlock. Physical (Phase 2 rules, no party signatures in a custodial wallet): Mark shipped takes the claim address and the password, inserts the requested release and sets release_at; nothing is built until the order is completed or resolved for the vendor (081). The buyer's signature is replaced by Confirm receipt with a password step-up under the order row lock, rechecking shipped and undisputed; afterwards the buyer cannot dispute; this is the only escrow exception to pooledOnly. Otherwise the deadline releases it. Each watcher runs the deadline step for its own coin's orders, so Monero deadlines fire with ESCROW_BTC off. A decision for the buyer supersedes the unbuilt release. CHAIR RULING 2026-10-04 (QA-Xe F4): a Monero dispute decision is final once made, unlike Bitcoin's (changeable until final): the market builds and relays on its next pass, so a change window would last minutes; the XMR resolve form states the consequence before submission. No-ship cancel as Phase 2. Notes and notices name the coin (no Bitcoin wording on a Monero order).
- **P3-R8 Refunds and late deposits.** settleTerminal dispatches on the order's currency; refunds go to the address pinned at checkout, fee from the amount. A top-up after build is left out and returned by a stray refund after close; after close, deposits are refunded automatically until the wallet is destroyed, and after that the page says they cannot be recovered (ES-8).
- **P3-R9 Destruction (ES-7, 90 days).** Preconditions: destroy_after (last close + 90 days) passed, no open order or live settlement, balance and unlocked balance 0 across all accounts, nothing in the pool, the last settlement at 100 confirmations. Method: change_wallet_password to 32 random bytes never stored, store, close_wallet, seed_sealed set to NULL, destroyed_at recorded; the app has no access to the wallet volume, so /admin lists destroyed wallet files for the operator to delete, and the docs say backups keep copies until they expire. A restored row past destroy_after is destroyed again (its restored seal wiped); one not yet due that cannot be opened becomes missing and alerts staff.
- **P3-R10 Restore.** scripts/restore.sh holds built and relayed Monero settlements (the restoredSettlementHold pattern), retires every free slot, moves every active wallet to closing and sets xmr_escrow_sweep_required (checkout 503 until the sweep). The sweep probes escrow-YYYYMM-NN names newer than any row (an orphan is re-sealed and its funded accounts listed for staff) and, for a wallet older than the database, sets the subaddress lookahead or creates accounts up to the highest slot before refreshing.
- **P3-R11 Custody copy.** OWNER DECISION 2026-10-03 (O-2, A-402): every Monero escrow page, notice and doc uses "held by this market in an account used only for this order, inside a wallet the market opens each month; the market can move these coins" (the ES-7 sentence "a wallet used only for this order" is retired).
- **P3-R12 PAYE-E2.** Measured on fakechain: one wallet with 10,000 accounts and 200 funded; times for create_account, open_wallet, refresh over 100 and 1,000 blocks, get_transfers all_accounts (time and size), incoming_transfers for one account, wallet size and wallet-rpc memory, and a seed restore with lookahead; pass when a full pass over 5 such wallets fits watcherPassTimeout and the poll interval. Report under artifacts/real-chain/paye-e2-<sha>.txt; the public-stagenet repeat is Phase 4.
- **P3-R13 Configuration.** ESCROW_XMR (on, off; blank is off), ESCROW_XMR_WALLET_RPC_URL, ESCROW_XMR_DAEMON_URL, ESCROW_XMR_FEE_BPS and ESCROW_XMR_FEE_ADDRESS (the fee rule matches Bitcoin escrow, A-232), ESCROW_XMR_SLOTS, ESCROW_XMR_MAX_FEE (P3-R6), MONERO_WALLET_SECRET, ESCROW_XMR_SEED_KEY. With ESCROW_XMR off nothing reads the new tables and the pooled Monero wallet is unchanged.

**Slices (waves; QA per slice on Opus for X-a to X-f):**
- **X-a (Opus; wave 1):** migration 084 (P3-R1) and the coin filters in Bitcoin escrow code. Tests TestXMREscrowMigration084 (each trigger and CHECK), TestEscrowBTCIgnoresXMRRows (every Bitcoin escrow pass and page with Monero rows present), TestXMREscrowPayoutRefused, TestXMREscrowIdxNoCollision. Mutants: drop one coin filter; drop the 081 check from the tx_hash trigger; allow a second built row; key idx on the minor index.
- **X-b (Opus; wave 1, parallel):** escrow_xmr_rpc.go (adapter, nettype and height checks, exact params), xmr_address.go (offline parsing), escrow_xmr_seal.go (password derivation, verifier, seed sealing and key pin), configuration in app.go. Tests: httptest pins of every call (account_index never 0 or absent), address vectors (valid, bad checksum, integrated, wrong network byte), seal round trip with a test key, secret refusals. Mutants: account_index 0 or omitted; password from SETUP_TOKEN; mainnet or stagenet nettype accepted; canary key used; checksum skipped.
- **X-c (Opus; wave 2):** escrow_xmr_watch.go (wallet lifecycle, slots, funding), escrow_xmr_create.go (checkout claim), the Monero order partial; scripts/test-escrow-xmr-regtest.sh with legs Wallets, Funding, TwoAccountsOneTx. Tests TestXMREscrowSlotClaimRace (100 concurrent checkouts), TestXMREscrowNoRPCAtCheckout, TestXMREscrowAccountZeroNeverSpent, TestXMREscrowSecretMismatch, TestXMREscrowSeedKeyRepinRefused. Mutants: slot claimable before the seal; RPC at checkout; credited below 10 confirmations; account 0 offered.
- **X-d (Opus; wave 3):** escrow_xmr_settle.go, settleTerminal dispatch, recovery hooks (P3-R6, R8); regtest legs RestartMidSend, RelayDropped, TopUp, Reorg, External. the administrator re-request (A-401). Tests TestXMREscrowStoredBeforeRelay, TestXMREscrowNeverRebuilt, TestXMREscrowReRequestGuards (refused while in the pool, on chain or with any key image spent; same key images and plan; step-up; superseded atomically), TestXMREscrowAmbiguousRelay, TestXMREscrowFreezeExcludesTopUp, TestXMREscrowPrimaryAddressMismatch, TestXMREscrowGatePauses. Mutants: relay before store; fee cap or claimant floor removed; rebuild on error; re-request with a spent key image or a changed destination; no freeze; no primary-address check; subset instead of equality on key images; no gate check.
- **X-e (Opus; wave 3, parallel):** escrow_xmr_release.go, the deadline step split by coin, the Confirm receipt edge, decisions (P3-R7); regtest leg Physical. Tests TestXMREscrowNoBuildBeforeOutcome, TestXMREscrowConfirmReceiptStepUp, TestXMREscrowDeadlineWithBTCOff, TestXMREscrowDisputeSupersedes, TestXMREscrowNoShipCancel. Mutants: Confirm receipt without step-up; no dispute recheck; build at shipped; deadline only with ESCROW_BTC on.
- **X-f (Opus; wave 4):** retention and destruction, restore (P3-R9, R10): escrow_xmr_retain.go, scripts/restore.sh, scripts/test-backup-restore.sh; regtest legs Restore, Lookahead, WrongSecret, Destroy. Tests TestXMREscrowRestoreRetiresSlots, TestXMREscrowDestroyPreconditions, TestXMREscrowRestoredPastDestroy, and TestXMREscrowRestoreNoUnrecordedSlot (after a restore no unrecorded account of a restored wallet ever becomes a free slot, even when the gate is cleared before the watcher's first pass: restore.sh moves active wallets to closing and reconcile never adds free slots to a wallet that is not active; QA-Xc). Mutants: destroy with a balance or a pool entry; free slots not retired; no lookahead; seal kept after destruction.
- **X-g1 (Sonnet; wave 5):** docs: docs/escrow.md Phase 3 section with evidence tiers, the operator guide (setup, seed key, secret, destruction, restore), testnet runbook, implementation status, acceptance, UPGRADING for each new migration, README, canary records.
- **X-g2 (Opus; wave 5, parallel):** the simulated escrow wallet-rpc fixture and tests/e2e/escrow-xmr-journey.spec.ts (320 px, JavaScript off, keyboard): checkout with a refund address and the custody copy, funding, digital claim, refund, Confirm receipt, deadline, dispute, restore-paused and destroyed-wallet states; the PAYE-E2 leg and report (P3-R12).

Gate for the Phase 3 PR: every commit builds and vets; race DB suite; Python; govulncheck; Chromium preview and DB; escrow journeys (Bitcoin and Monero); scripts/test-escrow-regtest.sh (Bitcoin, unchanged legs) and scripts/test-escrow-xmr-regtest.sh on monerod and monero-wallet-rpc 0.18.5.1 fakechain (hard fork >= 16, ring 16 decoded); test-upgrade.sh through the last new migration; test-backup-restore.sh; gitleaks; the CI Go job within its limit (A-400). Not covered and not claimed: stagenet or testnet escrow, mainnet, multisig Monero, wallets other than Bitcoin Core for Bitcoin.

### A-255 / A-249 ruling (chair, 2026-10-05) — architect, payments, security, infrastructure (Opus), devil's advocate (Sonnet); Rounds 0, A, B, C

Scope: A-255 (wallets beyond Bitcoin Core) and A-249 (Phase 4: public test networks, private broadcast, cutover, pooled removal). Evidence pack and the seats' reports are council-local (scratchpad council-a255/); the probes cited below ran on this host against the local bitcoin/bitcoin:31.1 image with no outside network, plus Electrum 4.8.2 (signature verified) used as an offline library. Tier: real local chain (regtest or private signet) and code reading. Nothing here is public-network evidence; P2P to public nodes is blocked from this environment.

Facts the council established (each reproduced by at least two seats unless noted):
- Private broadcast (Core 31.1). `-privatebroadcast` with no proxy and `-listen=0` refuses to start; with default listen (torcontrol) it starts and `sendrawtransaction` refuses (RPC -1); with `-proxy` or `-onion` pointing at a dead port it starts, `sendrawtransaction` returns the txid, nothing reaches the mempool and `getprivatebroadcastinfo` lists the tx with no peers; a resend is idempotent. No configuration broadcasts from the host's IP. Wallet sends (`sendtoaddress`, pooled payouts) are never private. `getnetworkinfo`'s onion "reachable" reflects configuration, not liveness. Escrow today records such a queued tx as broadcast (escrow_cosign.go:593-607, :652-667) and resends it each pass.
- The pooled RPC user can export the market key: with `rpcwhitelistdefault=0` and a whitelist for the escrow user only (docs/operator-guide.md:730-745), `listdescriptors true` on the arbiter wallet returns 403 to the escrow user and the tprv to the pooled user (security and infrastructure probes). Core selects the wallet by path; whitelists are per user, not per wallet.
- Core's `initialblockdownload` latches false: a node that lost its peers keeps IBD false and headers = blocks while deposits are invisible (payments probe, two regtest nodes). Today a paid escrow order is then cancelled "payment window expired" after the window (escrow_watch.go:311-326, chair-verified) and its coins come back only by stray refund; the pooled guard reads only IBD and tip regression (payments_bitcoin.go:87-135), so pooled public-testnet markets have the same hole today.
- A private signet needs no fetched code: `-chain=signet -signetchallenge=51` with Core's own `generatetoaddress <n> <tb1…> 2000000000` mines (default maxtries silently returns `[]`), reports chain "signet" and `tb1` addresses; a spend confirmed after 101 blocks (about 9 minutes; infrastructure). Its genesis equals public signet's; `getblockchaininfo` exposes `signet_challenge`.
- Electrum: a BIP39-style root keystore at m/48h/1h/0h/2h/i signs a per-order (shape (b)) escrow PSBT end to end with the market signing through Core `descriptorprocesspsbt` (combine, finalize, testmempoolaccept, broadcast, 1 confirmation; payments probe log). Electrum writes derivation entries for all three keys, so today's ES-2 paste check refuses every Electrum signature; that refusal is inert because a paste is never merged (checkSettlementPaste returns signatures only; withPartySigs rebuilds from the stored copy). Electrum's own seed type (what "Create a new seed" makes, the Tails default) fixes multisig derivation at m/1' (keystore.py:1220-1226, wizard.py:289-291; source reading by three seats, not executed): it cannot follow any per-order path the server derives.
- Pooled tables are read by escrow permanently: the refund, fee, vendor-destination and Monero address-reuse refusals read payment_addresses (escrow_create.go:292, escrow_fee.go:110, escrow_release.go:75, escrow_xmr_create.go:210; chair-verified); order history reads payouts (orders_load.go:138).
- Intake pause blocks escrow too (lockPaymentIntake is called by payAction and both escrow checkouts); unconfiguring the pooled provider stops the pooled watcher (payments_watcher.go:137) while pooled orders are open.

Rulings:

- **P4-R1 Split the gate (DA-1). DECISION: ACCEPT.** A-255 no longer blocks A-249: public test networks proceed with Bitcoin Core signing (shape (a)). WHY: the shape-(b) audience is BIP39-seed Electrum, Sparrow and (unverified) hardware users, not the Tails default; nothing in Phase 4 needs shape (b). DISSENTING VIEW: none after Round C (payments built shape (b) evidence and agrees it waits). USER IMPACT: Tails/Electrum users still cannot use escrow; docs say so.
- **P4-R2 Shape (b) (A-255). DECISION: DEFER (owner decision).** Recorded design if the owner builds it: per-order xpub account/i with i >= 2 from the one shared counter, enforced by a CHECK on escrow_pubkeys and the Go origin check plus an "no served xpub is an ancestor of another key" test (ARCH, PAY, DA, SEC final; SEC's interim account/2/i withdrawn); cosigner xpubs served rooted (depth 0, fingerprint 0, child 0); a dedicated socket-only signer with a fresh seed signing through Core `descriptorprocesspsbt` with ALL hard-coded and its own whitelisted user, refusing anything but one 2-of-3 P2WSH escrow per request (SEC-4, PAY-2); its seed is a second backup item; Go per-order derivation with math/big behind a Core cross-check that refuses on mismatch (crypto/elliptic is the wrong curve); the watch wallet and signer cover the per-order wallet's sibling addresses /0/0..19 and /1/0..9 so a top-up to /0/1 is refunded, not stranded (SEC MISSED); setup material party-only; the ES-2 derivation refusal is relaxed in that slice (P4-R13); hardware-wallet claims only with recorded matrix rows. DISSENTING VIEW: payments (Round B) wanted the Go derivation and paste relaxation built now; conceded in Round C.
- **P4-R3 Watcher sync guard (ARCH-1, PAY-6, PAY MISSED). DECISION: ACCEPT, P1, precondition of opening public networks; also a pooled safety fix now.** Stateless (no stored high-water mark: A-147's stall). A node counts as not synced when initialblockdownload is true, or headers > blocks, or (public chains only) connections = 0 (getnetworkinfo), or (public chains only) the tip block's time is older than BITCOIN_MAX_TIP_AGE (default 2h, minimum 30m, operator-adjustable). While not synced the escrow Bitcoin pass is skipped (no expiry, no spent_outside, no regression flags, no builds or broadcasts) and the pooled pass is skipped as today's IBD guard does; one log line per state change and a line under Payment providers naming the reason. Regtest keeps today's behaviour. DISSENTING VIEW: severity — architect and payments argued BLOCKER, security and devil's advocate REQUIRED; the ordering is the same (A-443 cannot merge without it). Bound: infrastructure and devil's advocate wanted 3h, architect 6h for testnet4; 2h default with the knob (a false positive only delays a negative decision). TECHNICAL COST/RISK: getnetworkinfo joins the escrow whitelist (read-only).
- **P4-R4 Chain identity (ARCH-6, PAY-5, INFRA-5, DA-2). DECISION: MODIFY.** A settings pin of the escrow node's chain name, plus signet_challenge when the chain is signet; refused with escrow disabled (fixed log line, admin line naming both values) when it differs while any escrow exists. A database never changes chain once it holds an escrow; a new chain needs a new database (mirrors invariant 2 and checkNetworkHistory). Startup backfill in Go: escrows exist and no pin → pin only when the node reports regtest, otherwise refuse. No per-escrow column. Chair modification: no genesis hash — getblockchaininfo carries both values, and the genesis hash duplicates the chain name for main/test/testnet4/signet while separating neither custom signets nor regtest instances (so getblockhash is not whitelisted). DISSENTING VIEW: payments (Round B) wanted escrows.chain NOT NULL; withdrawn in Round C.
- **P4-R5 Address and chain table (DA-2, ARCH-10, PAY-5). DECISION: ACCEPT.** One table maps chain to segwit HRP and versions (regtest bcrt; signet and testnet4 tb); escrow accepts regtest, signet and testnet4; main and testnet3 stay refused (ARCH-9; escrow hard-codes test key versions and coin type 1). regtestAddressScript callers (escrow_tx.go:118-141, checkFinalTx, escrow_bump.go:183, escrow_rpc.go:863) use the table.
- **P4-R6 Market key protection (INFRA-3, INFRA-1). DECISION: ACCEPT, precondition of opening public networks; BLOCKER for any mainnet opt-in.** A startup self-check: through the pooled client, `listdescriptors` (private=false) on the arbiter wallet path must answer 403 (or wallet-not-found, meaning another node), otherwise escrow stays disabled with a fixed log and admin line; the operator guide whitelists the pooled user to exactly the methods payments_bitcoin.go calls, or puts escrow on its own node; a documented, rehearsed arbiter and watch wallet backup and restore (operator user, encrypted to the operator key, block height recorded, importdescriptors fallback, fingerprint pin check), never inside the database backup; a doc line that escrow and pooled on one node share one failure domain. DISSENTING VIEW: devil's advocate preferred refusing when ESCROW_BTC_RPC_URL equals BITCOIN_RPC_URL; rejected (two URLs can reach one node; test the property, not the string).
- **P4-R7 Broadcast honesty and Tor-mode routing (PRIV-15; SEC-1, PAY-9, INFRA-6, DA-9, ARCH-14). DECISION: ACCEPT.** (a) A stall alert in every mode, derived from existing data (state broadcast, the watch wallet has not seen the txid after N blocks): a staff notice and an admin line; no new settlement state (074 CHECK); getprivatebroadcastinfo optional read-only; abortprivatebroadcast never whitelisted. (b) In APP_MODE=tor only, broadcasts wait (admin line) unless getnetworkinfo shows an ipv4 and ipv6 proxy and version >= 310100, evaluated once per pass; operator-guide.md:56 stops saying APP_MODE changes no behaviour. (c) A Compose node overlay with a separate tor-socks service (its own torrc, SocksPort reachable only from the node network; the onion container keeps SocksPort 0), bitcoind `-proxy=tor-socks:9050 -privatebroadcast` (never -connect or -onlynet), monerod proxy flags documented as unverified; docs state pooled payouts never leave privately. DISSENTING VIEW: devil's advocate wanted the alert only (the gate checks configuration, not liveness); ruled both because the gate prevents the irreversible leak and the alert covers liveness.
- **P4-R8 Cutover lever (ARCH-8, SEC-10). DECISION: ACCEPT.** payment_intake_<COIN> gains a third value 'escrow': lockPaymentIntake refuses pooled callers (409, no payment_addresses row) and admits escrow; the same refusal in the draft to awaiting_payment guard (orders_state.go:194); the admin form works with no pooled provider, step-up and audit as today; reversible; older binaries read it as paused. A readiness panel counts active listings that are not escrow-eligible and vendors without an active key; such listings stay visible with a plain "not available for purchase" reason (ARCH-3). DISSENTING VIEW: devil's advocate's configuration-only cutover (DA-8) withdrawn; infrastructure's separate switch conceded.
- **P4-R9 Drain gate (ARCH-2, PAY-10, SEC-11, INFRA-7). DECISION: ACCEPT.** A SQL function pooled_drain_blockers() listing: open pooled orders; payouts not sent (with ARCH-12: a blocked payout can be recorded sent by hand with a txid, step-up and audit); flagged pooled payments with disposition '' or 'escalated' (closed through the existing disposition, which is the acknowledgement); pooled payment_addresses inside the watch horizon counted from issuance and order-terminal time (payments_watcher.go:252-254); payments_recovery_required; unresolved 071 collisions; mainnet-class pooled history while mainnet escrow does not exist. A read-only drain panel shows it, with the pooled wallet balance as advice and a sweep note. The refusing migration lands with the removal release. DISSENTING VIEW: security and payments proposed a new administrator acknowledgement and an attested balance; both withdrawn.
- **P4-R10 Releases (INFRA-7). DECISION: MODIFY.** After the public-network release, two code releases: (1) the 'escrow' lever (off by default) and the drain panel ship with Phase 4; the cutover is an administrator flip, not a release; (2) the removal release deletes pooled writers only (payAction's pooled path, the pooled poll and send, the providers), keeps the tables, readers and order history permanently, carries a 069-style migration that RAISEs naming each blocker and changes nothing (a restored older database re-runs it), refuses a mainnet-class database (ARCH-9), refuses to start with pooled settings present, parses network configuration once (ARCH-7), and moves shared helpers out of pooled files first (ARCH-11). DISSENTING VIEW: infrastructure keeps three releases because the drain wait is real time; the wait sits between the flip and release (2), so it needs no third release.
- **P4-R11 Real-chain legs in CI (DA-3). DECISION: ACCEPT, P1, first.** A CI job runs scripts/test-escrow-regtest.sh against bitcoin/bitcoin:31.1 before the Phase 4 refactors touch escrow_tx.go and the watcher.
- **P4-R12 Reproducible wallet evidence (DA-5, PAY-12, SEC EXP-1). DECISION: ACCEPT.** E1 and the Round A/B probes become a retained script, never in CI.
- **P4-R13 ES-2 derivation refusal. DECISION: MODIFY.** Keep it until the first slice that admits a non-Core wallet; add now a regression test proving it is inert (only the party's signatures reach the signer and the database). DISSENTING VIEW: payments, architect and infrastructure (Round B) would relax it now; devil's advocate and security would not change a Round D-tested refusal with no consumer; all converged in Round C.
- **P4-R14 Mainnet fee descriptor (ES-5; SEC-7, PAY-8, ARCH-13). DECISION: DEFER (owner confirmation).** Escrow cannot run on mainnet; the descriptor (strict Go parse, sealed, index allocated at the first release build and reused on rebuild, never at checkout) becomes the acceptance criterion of a separate mainnet-escrow item.
- **P4-R15 Monero on stagenet. DECISION: DEFER (owner decision).** If included: the accepted address tags follow the verified nettype (stagenet 24/36; fakechain 18/42) in the same commit that relaxes checkDaemon (PAY-7), the height heuristic is replaced by a pin, a Monero sync guard (synchronized plus top block timestamp), a Compose escrow wallet-rpc service (INFRA-11), tested on the offline stagenet fork (docs/local-chain-testing.md). Pooled removal (release (2)) cannot remove pooled Monero before Monero escrow works on the market's network.
- **P4-R16 Public-network evidence. DECISION: BLOCK RELEASE of the cutover recommendation until done; route is an owner decision.** A trimmed operator smoke (practice-sign, one funded escrow claimed to confirmation, one refund) writing a manifest (full commit, image digests, chain and signet_challenge, start and end heights, txids per leg, log sha256, no secrets) to a tracked path with a secret scan (INFRA-4, INFRA-8 trimmed), plus an escrow node bootstrap script (INFRA-2). Never weaken the regtest scripts' guards to point them at public nodes.

Slices (ledger rows A-438–A-452): wave 1 = A-438 (CI), A-439 (node identity and sync guard: P4-R3, R4, R5), A-450 (wallet evidence script and the inertness test: P4-R12, R13); then A-440 (market key protection), A-441 (broadcast honesty and Tor-mode gate), A-442 (Tor node overlay), A-443 (open signet and testnet4 plus the private-signet harness), A-444 (cutover lever and readiness), A-445 (drain gate and hand settlement), A-446 (operator package); blocked: A-447 (public run), A-448 (removal release), A-449 (Monero stagenet, owner), A-451 (escrow health panel, P2), A-452 (arbiter key HMAC pin, SEC-9, P2).

### Owner decisions on the A-255 / A-249 ruling (2026-10-05) and the chair's follow-on rulings

Owner answers (AskUserQuestion, 2026-10-05):
- **Wallets beyond Core (A-255): "Build per-order wallets now".** P4-R2 changes from DEFER to ACCEPT: shape (b) is built in Phase 4 to the design P4-R2 records (rows A-453–A-457). DISSENTING VIEW kept: devil's advocate (DA-6) and security (Round C) recommended Core only for the launch because Electrum's own seed type, the Tails default, cannot follow a per-order path; the docs and the in-app copy must say plainly that shape (b) needs a BIP39 seed in Electrum, Sparrow, or (unverified) a hardware wallet.
- **Monero on stagenet: "Include it".** A-449 opens (P4-R15's design); it also closes the pooled Monero peer-loss hole A-439 found (payments_monero.go reads only target_height).
- **Public run: "You run the script".** A-446 builds the package; A-447 is the owner's run on their host.
- **Mainnet fee destination: "Move it".** ES-5's descriptor becomes the acceptance of a future mainnet-escrow item (A-460); A-249 no longer includes it.
- **Disk:** the owner approved clearing the Go build cache now and whenever free disk drops below 5 GB during Phase 4.

Chair rulings that follow (the owner may override any of them):
- **P4-R17 Signer custody defaults. DECISION: ACCEPT (chair default).** A fresh seed (never the Core arbiter's), in a mode-0400 file in the signer's own volume, never in .env, the database or scripts/backup.sh output, never derived from or sealed by SETUP_TOKEN; `escrow-signer backup` writes it PGP-encrypted to the operator key; restore must match the pin. The signer listens only on a Unix socket mounted into the app, signs through Core descriptorprocesspsbt with SIGHASH_ALL hard-coded under its own rpcauth user whitelisted to descriptorprocesspsbt (and getdescriptorinfo if needed), never returns key material, and refuses: any sighash but ALL; any input that is not P2WSH `2 <3 sorted distinct compressed keys> 3 CHECKMULTISIG` with witness_utxo matching; inputs with differing scripts (one escrow per request); a script without exactly its key for the requested order index at /0/k or /1/k with k < 20 (sibling addresses, P4-R2); signing any other key. The app pins the signer's account fingerprint and an HMAC of its account xpub; a mismatch disables shape (b) only; a signer that is down makes settlements wait (no rebuild). A documented break-glass procedure signs from the backup offline. The Core arbiter wallet stays for shape (a). WHY: SEC-4, PAY-2, INFRA's operator requirements; ARCH-5 retracted. DISSENTING VIEW: none after Round C; passphrase-at-rest (co-signing stops until unlocked) was the rejected alternative because it breaks auto-delivery and the 14-day release.
- **P4-R18 Mixed pairs. DECISION: ACCEPT (chair default).** Each user keeps one active key (user_escrow_keys is keyed by user) with a layout, core or per_order; an escrow is shape (b) only when both parties' active keys are per_order and shape (a) only when both are core; a mixed checkout is refused with a plain message naming the vendor's key type, which the listing also shows. A user switches layout by registering a new key and passing the practice signature again (ES-9). WHY: a Core wallet cannot sign a per-order key without exporting a private key (payments, security); a second active key per user is a later option if asked. DISSENTING VIEW: none; payments' OQ-2 and security's Q5 recommended the same.
- **P4-R19 A-439 readings. DECISION: ACCEPT both (QA4W1 judged them).** (a) The IBD and headers > blocks checks apply on regtest too (only the peer and tip-age checks are public-chain only): P4-R3's wording, the pooled guard's existing behaviour, and only liveness is at stake; QA4W1 showed a regtest node restarted with a tip older than 24 h reports IBD until a block is mined, so the docs and admin line name that remedy (QA4W1-2). (b) Release deadlines still run while the node is not synced: escrowDeadlines makes no RPC and only moves shipped to completed; build, co-sign and broadcast happen only in the pass steps the guard skips (QA4W1 scratch test: two not-synced passes made only getblockchaininfo calls; the next synced pass co-signed and broadcast).
- **A-491 Public Monero evidence package. DECISION: ACCEPT, with the reviewers' corrections; DEFER a multi-day fresh cloud sync.** WHY: the existing offline stagenet fork cannot fund an escrow at the supported hard fork. DISSENTING VIEW: the source reviewer rejected a supplied explorer URL/hash as independent public provenance; the chair accepted the finding and required an actual HTTPS explorer fetch in both runner and Go test. Real daemon probes also disproved the mocked urllib Digest compatibility claim; curl now passes with private stdin credentials and default no-redirect behavior. USER IMPACT: no production payment or authorization change. OPERATOR IMPACT: a dedicated synced public daemon, funded test wallet, empty escrow wallet store, disposable database and available explorer are required; no public result is written on a refusal. TECHNICAL COST/RISK: three isolated test/harness files plus documentation, no relaxed offline or mainnet guards. ACCEPTANCE/TEST PLAN: eight Python record/refusal checks, cloud Go compilation/refusal test and actual authenticated daemon calls passed; funded deposit/build/relay/confirmation/received-amount proof remains pending. A single digital-claim timing sample does not establish refund timing or validate A-482's 24-hour threshold.


| A-490 | Current runtime security gate: Go 1.27.1 triggers 13 newly published standard-library advisories in CI 37954442970. Update the supported minimum to 1.26.9, toolchain and Docker build to 1.27.2, and current setup docs. Preserve historical verification versions. ACCEPTANCE: module verification, vet, clean govulncheck and full CI with patched builds; public operator smoke records its actual patched runtime. | infrastructure | chair, actual CI finding 2026-10-09 | verified (pins 79f2517, integrated 4676033; Go 1.27.2 module verification/vet/govulncheck passed with no affecting vulnerabilities; all required checks passed in CI 37957076020 at 9885bee (22 CI jobs; GitGuardian neutral); public operator runtime is captured separately by A-447) |

| A-261 | QA18e-1 (REQUIRED, reopens A-155): a same-fingerprint key update keeps the proof when a revocation signature is merely moved (e.g. a subkey revocation placed straight after the primary key): `revocationSignatures` compares raw packet bytes while go-crypto ignores a misplaced revocation, so the saved key selects the revoked subkey for encryptTo and stays "Verified" (reproduced with real gpg keys; at 6dac203 the same paste cleared the proof). Compare revocation STATE: every subkey or user ID revoked in the stored parse must be revoked (or absent) in the pasted parse; keep the byte check. | A-320 | The chain run's Playwright JSON report (the A-103 manifest's `playwright-report.json`) embeds `webServer.env`, including the local RPC passwords and `DATABASE_URL`; 668100d's copy was redacted by hand after the run and nothing in the repo warns about it (A-103 implementer finding) | tests/ops | implementer finding; chair read of Playwright 1.63 runner (web server env = defaults, then process.env, then webServer.env; only webServer.env is serialised) | P3, ruled: fold into the A-103 rework — playwright.chain.config.ts puts chainEnv and DATABASE_URL into process.env and keeps only non-secret settings in webServer.env, and the spec's `sql()` passes the connection string to psql through its environment (PGDATABASE), not its arguments (a failing psql call would otherwise print the URL into the reports); proof: a grep of the new JSON report for each runtime.json password and the database URL finds nothing, with no post-run redaction, and a deliberately failing query in a throwaway run leaves no URL in its report; docs/local-chain-testing.md documents the manifest procedure. verified (PR #33 @dd777b8, CI 36746860618; QA30: the evidence run's JSON report and HTML report, its embedded zip decoded, hold none of the 3 RPC passwords, the database password, postgres://, the database name, PGPASSWORD or DATABASE_URL, with nothing redacted; a throwaway run with a failing sql() query prints only the psql arguments and "relation does not exist"). Implementation (1c62647, merged 6f53303): credentials reach the server through process.env; `sql()` sets PGHOST/PGPORT/PGUSER/PGPASSWORD/PGDATABASE/PGSSLMODE (psql 16 does not expand a URI in PGDATABASE, so the chair's suggestion was replaced); a throwaway run with a forced failing query left no password or database URL in the JSON report, the HTML report (with its embedded zip), the log or browser results; the evidence run's grep found 0 hits with no redaction |

| A-322 | Escrow only (local escrow/phase1): while escrow is off or unavailable the watcher records no deposit, so an escrow order that shows none can still be cancelled even if coins already reached its escrow address; the operator guide now says so (A-314–A-319 implementer's open question) | payments/escrow | implementer finding | verified (QA35 at ab77a46: cancelled during an outage → stray refund to the refund address after restart; Recover refusals and the daily check). fixed (local escrow/phase1 @ab77a46, P1-c2 35a6274: "Recover coins at this escrow" with password step-up plus a daily check, to the pinned refund address; regtest stray refund 1ceed18a…12db; QA35 pending). Chair ruling: do not block the cancel (a buyer must not be trapped by a node outage, and the coins stay in a 2-of-3 address the buyer co-controls). Carry-over into P1-c's acceptance: the buyer stray refund ("Recover coins at this escrow", daily check plus on demand) covers an escrow cancelled while escrow was unavailable, and the cancel form, while escrow is unavailable, says that coins already sent stay at the escrow address and are recoverable there. QA31 describes today's behaviour first QA31 (today's behaviour on real Core): with the flag off, 0.001 BTC sent to an awaiting escrow and the buyer's cancel went through; when escrow came back the deposit was recorded after_close with no notice to anyone and no payment-review flag; no in-app route recovers it (buyer+vendor co-signing from their own wallets works on regtest). AMENDED by the chair (QA31-3 → A-327): still no block on the cancel (with the flag off a block would lock escrow orders for good), but the after-close notice and the cancel-form warning move into P1-b fix round 2; the stray refund stays in P1-c. |

| A-400 | CI Go job near its limit (SHOULD, ops/ci): PR #37's CI run spent 17m17s of the job's 20 minutes, with go test -race -timeout 17m serial over internal/market; Phase 3 adds DB tests, so the next PR would time out. Measure the slowest tests on a timed race run, cut wasted time (sleeps, repeated setups, missing t.Parallel where safe) before raising any limit; never skip or weaken a test | ops | chair (CI run on PR #37) | verified (PR #38, three CI runs of the same Go code: the Go and PostgreSQL job took 11m23s (37189221425 on 7d8d128, test step 10m23s), 14m56s (37192638933, step 14m07s, failed on A-433) and 15m56s (37194533142 on 1d0dc7c, step 14m34s) of its 20 minutes, against PR #37's 17m17s; runner speed varies by about 40%, so internal/market stays under about 815 s of the 1020 s limit and the job about 4 minutes inside its own, roughly 20% headroom that the next phase's tests should be measured against; no test skipped or weakened). fixed (escrow/phase3 d0b3ca6, ci/a400 fd8e3ae): each DB test copies a per-process template database migrated once by migrate (CREATE DATABASE TEMPLATE, WAL_LOG), falling back to a schema per test without CREATEDB; TestNewTestAppMatchesAFreshStart compares a copy with a fresh start (schema and every row). Dedicated PostgreSQL 17, race: internal/market 798 s to 582 s on the same tests; with X-a and X-b merged 660 s, wall 690 s (main before Phase 3: 765 s, 828 s); no test newly skipped; fallback run passed with a NOCREATEDB role. CI timing confirmed on the Phase 3 PR. With all Phase 3 code merged (f31d213) internal/market took 1053 s under heavy load (two QA passes and a journey run alongside), above CI's -timeout 17m; quiet runs on earlier merges took 599 to 680 s. A quiet measurement on the final tree decides before the PR whether CI needs more. Final quiet measurement on c6b11ce with CI's exact command (go test -race -count=1 -timeout 17m -coverprofile, 4 cores, nothing else running): internal/market 817 s, wall 848 s. PR #37's CI test step took 967 s for main, which took 828 s wall locally, so the projection is about 990 s for the step (internal/market about 895 s of the 1020 s limit, job about 17m40s of 20). Limits kept; the Phase 3 PR's CI timing confirms or reopens. A killed or panicking run leaves its template (documented); a clean run leaves none (checked) |
| A-401 | Owner question O-1 (payments; escrow only): ES-7 says a Monero settlement is never rebuilt. Default until answered: no re-request path; failed flags staff | payments | payments seat (Phase 3 design) | ruled (OWNER DECISION 2026-10-03: allow the administrator re-request with the guards above; built in X-d) |

| A-415 | Chair ruling on X-d (payments/schema; escrow only, local): X-d stored why a Monero settlement waits or failed, and each administrator re-request's state, as per-row keys in the key-value settings table (no FK, no constraints, unbounded growth, unlike Bitcoin's settlements.send_error, where the restore hold also writes). Ruled: typed storage in migration 085 (xmr_settlements.send_error; table xmr_settlement_rerequests with forward-only states, one open request per settlement, deletes refused); no per-settlement settings keys. Accepted as X-d chose: a settlement claims deposits counted at the threshold (an underpaid order's deposits are never credited, so the literal rule could never refund them; deposits still confirming are left out and stray-refunded), and an underpaid order cancels to request its refund, as Bitcoin escrow does. Accepted as X-e chose: a separate escrow_xmr_release_recheck setting, so each watcher consumes only its own coin's recheck after a restore | payments | chair (X-d, X-e reports) | verified (final cross-slice QA on f31d213: 085 as ruled, no per-settlement settings keys, classification and set-aside as described (M19 killed); the shared payments_rpc.go change leaves pooled and Bitcoin text, classification and send recording unchanged and a timeout is never dropped (M8, M8b killed); the pooled log class NIT is A-427) |
| A-416 | QA-Xe (escrow only, local; no BLOCKER; 16 of 18 mutants killed, M15 equivalent): F1 the order page offered Ship, Confirm receipt and Claim with ESCROW_XMR off and each POST answered 404 (graded REQUIRED by the chair: a form that cannot work); F2 the Monero watcher returned before xmrDeadlines while the node was unreachable or refused, leaving a past-due shipped order neither released nor disputable (graded REQUIRED; Bitcoin applies deadlines when its node check fails); F3 the Physical regtest leg's zero-spend check was trivially true without a builder (now strengthened to prove build and relay after the outcome, X-d merged); F4 decision change ruled final for Monero (P3-R7); F5 the restore recheck's reminded_at untested (M17); F6 xmrSettlementBuildable unused (X-d calls settlementFits), XMR banner test window loose. QA also confirmed the gate equals 081 over 108 combinations and the recheck setting is consistent across restore.sh, UPGRADING section 6 and lateDispute | payments | QA (X-e, 60307c5) | verified (final cross-slice QA on f31d213: forms gated, deadlines while not ready (M16 killed), gate (M10 killed), resolve-form consequence) |
| A-417 | Integration (REQUIRED, payments; escrow only, local): offline Monero address parsing (xmr_address.go) checks base58, the Keccak checksum and the network byte but not that the spend and view keys are points on the ed25519 curve. On the merged tree, X-e's Physical regtest leg (synthetic refund addresses with valid checksums) made monero-wallet-rpc refuse each refund build with -2 WRONG_ADDRESS, every pass. A crafted refund or claim address is therefore accepted at checkout, claim or ship and its settlement can never be built; a mistyped ESCROW_XMR_FEE_ADDRESS that passes the checksum stops every settlement. Fix: decompress both keys in Go and require l·P = identity (prime-order subgroup; standard library math/big; no new dependency): X-d saw monero-wallet-rpc 0.18.5.1 segfault (exit 139) building a refund to a checksum-valid address whose random keys pass the wallet's own check_key, so a crafted refund address could crash the escrow wallet-rpc every pass and stop all Monero escrow; and refuse off-curve keys with their own message at every entry point and at startup for the fee address; test helpers generate on-curve keys; external vectors include known-good addresses and an off-curve key with a valid checksum | payments | chair (all 9 real fakechain legs on f37fda1: 8 pass, Physical fails on -2) | verified (final cross-slice QA on f31d213: xmrDecompress follows RFC 8032 5.1.3, edAdd 5.1.4, constants correct; applied at checkout, claim, ship (M9 killed), build destinations and the fee address at startup) |
| A-418 | Chair review of the X-c fix (SHOULD, payments; escrow only, local): on resume, when create_wallet answers -21 but open_wallet then fails (a file left by an earlier install under another password), xmrCreateWallet returns the error every pass: the row stays creating, which blocks this month's wallet, so Monero escrow checkout stays unavailable (no free slot) and staff are not told. A double fault (a crash between the row insert and create_wallet, plus a foreign file of that name). Fix in X-f with the orphan sweep: a resume whose open fails on the password (or any definite refusal) marks the row orphan, notifies admins once and moves on to the next wallet number; transport errors keep retrying | payments | chair (review of xmrWalletFresh and the resume path, a9583a9) | verified (closing QA re-check on 2ad9a84 and final QA on 9721307: locked files in the resume covered, M18 re-planted and killed by ResumeOpenFailsOrphan) |
