Chair accepted two additions: the guard also skips an order whose refund failed too-small (those deposits counted; leaving them out would relabel them and request a stray refund that fails the same way on every pass), and the order page says why the coins are held (status line and left-out deposit text, which wrongly said "nothing was received" and "after waiting 24 hours"). Probe failed on main; mutants a, b, b2, c, d, e killed; Core 31.1 regtest 11 of 11 on a fresh node (Core reported the replaced deposit at -1 confirmations and listreceivedbyaddress dropped it); full race suite ok 824.5 s. ruled (chair, 2026-10-04, owner: start A-428 after PR #38 merged as 665f766). Confirmed by reading: Bitcoin escrow records deposits through applyIncoming, so Core's negative confirmations for a conflicted transaction, or markMissing's -1 for one no longer listed, leave the payment window's d empty; the order is cancelled unpaid with no refund; once mined the row is neither after_close nor escrow_left_out, so strayEligibleSQL never selects it. leaveOutTopUps already covers an order whose release or refund is built (past building), so the gap is a cancelled order with no refund that spends or will spend its deposits. RULING, as A-424: the window rule stays; leaveOutUncounted (escrow_release.go beside leaveOutTopUps) marks, on a cancelled Bitcoin escrow order with no refund in building, awaiting_signature, party_signed, signed, broadcast or confirmed, every deposit with settlement_id NULL, confirmations at or above the threshold, not locked, after_close, escrow_left_out or spent_outside, as escrow_left_out and flagged, with one order note and one buyer notice (flagOrder tells staff), only for rows newly marked; settleEscrow calls it in its terminal branch before flagLateDeposits; the stray refund returns them. ACCEPTANCE: (1) probe first, failing on main: TestEscrowConflictedDepositThroughExpiry, a deposit conflicted (negative confirmations) and one no longer listed at window end, cancelled unpaid, then mined to the threshold: left out, flagged, one note and notice, a stray_refund to the pinned refund address requested; (2) a building refund keeps its deposits; a built refund's later deposit is left out once, by leaveOutTopUps, not noted twice; repeated passes notify once; completed and resolved orders untouched by the new path; (3) mutants killed: no cancelled-state check, no refund guard, no after_close exclusion, threshold 0; (4) a real Bitcoin Core 31.1 regtest leg in scripts/test-escrow-regtest.sh: a deposit replaced by a conflicting transaction that is mined, through window end (cancelled unpaid), then invalidateblock removes the replacement, the original deposit is mined to 3 confirmations, and the stray refund is requested, signed by the buyer's Core wallet, co-signed, broadcast and confirmed to the refund address; the transcript records what Core reports for the conflicted deposit; (5) docs/escrow.md and the operator guide say what happens to such a deposit; no migration; no test skipped or weakened |
| A-429 | PAYE-E2 finding (REQUIRED, payments/ops; escrow only, local): on real monerod and monero-wallet-rpc 0.18.5.1 fakechain (X-g2, report paye-e2-2a93cf716406.txt), a catch-up refresh over 110 blocks holding 200 deposits to one 10,000-account wallet took 2.5 min (747 ms per deposit received), over the 2-minute xmrWalletSlowTimeout given to refresh, so after downtime with many deposits the watcher's refresh times out every pass. Fix: refresh gets its own bound inside the 10-minute pass timeout and a timed-out refresh does not stop catch-up; experiment: a minor subaddress lookahead of 1 on escrow wallets (only address 0 of each account is used), kept only if measured several times faster with every real leg passing. Unchecked. Also unreturned: a refund that failed too-small plus a conflicted deposit that confirms later; QA428-1 reproduced the unreturned case (5000-sat counted deposit, refund failed too-small, 500000-sat deposit conflicted then mined: never left out, flagged or returned); QA428-5: the cancelled-order status still says held and returned after the stray refund is broadcast or confirmed (same pattern in the after-close status) | ui | implementer (A-428) | verified (final QA on b2a5650: QA435B-1 fixed (a refund's too-small hold ends once a stray refund spends a coin it left out; Q1, Q6 pass; Q2 two failures then held; new edge Q7 more than 50 deposits: one extra failure then held, same as the ordinary path), B-2, B-3, B-5, B-6 fixed; M2a, M2b, G1, X3, W1, W2 killed; G2, G3 accepted as equivalent; NIT-1 doc qualifier added by the chair; Escrow race suite 313 pass; gate at b2a5650: every commit builds and vets, Python 180, govulncheck 0 affecting, gitleaks clean, Chromium 362, escrow 8, escrow-xmr 8, wallet 6; transcripts a435fix2-4f4d6b9 Core 31.1 11/11 and monerod 0.18.5.1 14/14). QA re-check on b4f52ed: VERIFIED (REQUIRED-1, SHOULD-1, NIT-1..3 closed; A1, A3, M2a, M2c, M4c, M4e, B3, B5, C3, T1, X1, X2, X4 killed; Escrow race suite 311 pass; transcripts a435fix-a96fc67 11/11 and 14/14) with QA435B-1 (SHOULD, payments: a refund's too-small failure stays the latest word after a stray refund spent its coins, holding later refundable coins and showing a false status), QA435B-2 (SHOULD, tests: M2b ordering by updated survives), QA435B-3 (SHOULD, docs: "fail too-small once" not true in the same-pass case), NITs B-4 (upgraded-row note covers a conflicted deposit; ACCEPTED: upgrade-only, regtest-only data, wording), B-5 (X3 survives), B-6 (Monero status while a stray refund waits too-small). CHAIR: final round fixes B-1, B-2, B-3, B-5, B-6. NOT VERIFIED (QA on 225f104: behaviour verified for (1)-(5), Monero no-loop conclusion confirmed, transcripts 11/11 and 14/14; acceptance fails: REQUIRED-1 mutants A1 (leaveOutTooFew without its cancelled guard) and A3 (strayHeldSQL refund hold on any order state) survive the Escrow suite; SHOULD-1 a stray refund created before the cancelled order's refund fails makes the same coins fail too-small twice (latest row by id), contradicting the ruling and operator-guide.md:934; NIT-1 upgraded rows relabelled "did not count"; NIT-2 Monero cancelled status stale after the stray refund is sent; NIT-3 B3, B5, C3 and the Monero panel title untested). CHAIR: fix round covers all of them. Implementer commit f17a709, merge 225f104. ruled (chair, 2026-10-05, owner: A-435 and A-436 next after PR #39 merged as 9581c07). (1) Bitcoin, unreturned case: when a cancelled order's refund failed too-small, its counted deposits and any uncounted deposit that confirms later are left out (escrow_left_out, flagged, one note and notice) so a stray refund can return them together; the A-434 held rule also reads the latest too-small failure of kind refund (its input_total and fee_rate are already stored), so no stray refund is requested for the same coins (no second failure) until the value grows, the fee rate falls or the buyer asks. (2) Bitcoin order page: the cancelled-order and after-close statuses follow the stray refund's state (requested or awaiting signatures, broadcast, confirmed) instead of always saying held and returned. (3) The stray-refund panel title, Bitcoin and Monero, names every way in (coins that do not count for the order: sent after it closed, left out of its settlement, or not counted when it was cancelled). (4) Monero order page: a left-out deposit (A-424, or left out after a build) gets its own line and is not counted as received, and the cancelled status neither promises a refund the market builds nor says nothing was received. (5) Check first whether a Monero stray refund that fails for too few coins repeats every pass (the A-434 loop); if it does, fix it the same way in this round. ACCEPTANCE: a failing-first test for each of 1, 2, 4 (and 5 if reproduced); mutants killed for the new guards; non-held and unaffected page texts byte-identical; docs (escrow.md, operator-guide.md) updated where they describe these cases; no migration unless a constraint forbids it |
| A-436 | QA re-check observation on A-434 (SHOULD, payments; escrow only; pre-existing): a stray refund spends every eligible input, so at a high ESCROW_BTC_FEE_RATE a dust input that costs more to spend than it adds makes an otherwise refundable stray refund too-small (now one held failure instead of a loop) | payments | QA (8c1d74a) | verified (final gate at b2a5650). QA re-check on b4f52ed: VERIFIED (SHOULD-2 closed, D4, D5, D6 killed). VERIFIED with SHOULD-2 (QA on 225f104: failing-first, D1-D3 killed, cost equals the fee code's per-input term, plan and PSBT use the kept inputs; D4 boundary, D5 skip on refunds and releases, D6 whole-transaction cost survive). CHAIR: SHOULD-2 tests in the A-435 fix round. ruled (chair, 2026-10-05). A Bitcoin stray refund leaves out of its build any eligible input whose value is no more than its own spending cost at the current fee rate (the 2-of-3 P2WSH input size times the rate), as long as other inputs remain; skipped inputs stay eligible for a later stray refund, and the A-434 stored value stays the value of every eligible deposit, so a skip never looks like growth. If every input is uneconomic the build fails too-small as before. Releases and refunds are unchanged. ## Declined

| ID | Item | Why |
|---|---|---|
| QA27-2 | tests/test_script_cleanup.py accepts `-v=false` / `--volumes=false` and ignores `docker -H … rm`, `docker --context … rm` and `docker compose rm` | QA27 and chair: no such line exists in scripts/, nobody writes `-v=false` by accident, and each case adds parser complexity to guard a ~49 MB leak; the check catches the regression it exists for (dropping -v). Revisit only if a script adds one of these forms |
| D-1 | Admin "mark sent" being the only exit for some held payouts | conservative by design (Fable ruling) |
| D-2 | Tor onion reachability as a roadmap item | environment evidence, not code; track under ops verification |
| D-3 | A-14 auto-completion/abandonment timers | first unattended payout trigger, against the human-confirmed payout posture; abandonment already has a vendor-dispute path (`orders_state.go`) (DA-8, iteration 3) |
| D-4 | A-19 canary freshness indicator | page already shows signature and posted dates; freshness needs an operator policy (DA-10) |
| D-5 | Web unlock of `payments_recovery_required` | clearing it is an operator assertion after reconciliation; a button makes a false claim cheap (DA-11, QA3, MOD) |
| D-6 | Web "record an external payout" and mark-sent refusing unknown txids | a hijacked admin session could suppress a real payout (one settlement per order: `enqueuePayout` stops at any payout row; `UNIQUE (order_id, kind)` since migration 064); wallets restored from seed do not know later txids (PM-4 original, MOD-2 original; iteration 3) |
| D-7 | Global web payout pause | duplicates the restore gate and stopping the app; `op=hold` (A-34) covers the real case (MOD-4 revised) |
| D-9 | Per-party message delete (BUY9-8) | one shared row: deleting erases the counterparty's copy (dispute evidence), hiding leaves the ciphertext in the database and backups — a button promising an erasure that does not happen; security, privacy and frontend all rejected it; A-94 is the only shape that removes rows (iteration 9) |
| D-10 | Confirm checkbox for key user IDs that look like names/emails (SEC9-1 original) | withdrawn by security: fires on nearly every key, heuristic, no-JS round-trip loses the pasted key; A-79 disclosure instead (iteration 9) |
| D-11 | User account/data deletion ("erase me") | orders, payouts and audit rows underpin invariants 3, 4 and 8, and `auditExport` joins `users.handle`, so deleting/renaming changes past export bytes; A-81 disclosure is the proportionate answer for a test-network market (buyer, privacy NOT WORTH; iteration 9) |
| D-12 | Minimum age before requeueing an ambiguous payout (PAY9-2 original) | retracted by payments after the v0.18.5.1 source: a pool spend becomes pending on the next 20 s auto-refresh and wallet-rpc serves one request at a time; A-101's runbook commands close the gap (iteration 9) |
| D-13 | Full wallet/chain identity pinning (Monero primary address stored + Bitcoin `getaddressinfo ismine` + provider match) (PAY9-3 original) | over-engineered (payments itself, QA): Monero misattribution needs an in-process wallet swap; a recreated Bitcoin wallet draws fresh keys and fails safe; the real harm (silent expiry) is A-105 (iteration 9) |
| D-14 | Browser proof of every payment recovery journey with hang/drop/check-fail fixtures (QA9-3 original) | Go DB pins the server refusals; a hang case costs >30 s per case or a test knob in production (DA); cut to A-22 widened (staff, DA, QA) (iteration 9) |
| D-15 | Automatic re-point of held/failed payouts on address save (VEN10-1 original) | a stolen password re-points; definite-failed requeue is gated only by the admin's password — replaced by the admin action A-121 (iteration 10) |
| D-16 | Sign-out form in the header (PM10-8 original) | header already 529–707 px tall at 320 px/200% and an accidental POST one Tab stop after the skip link; moved to the top of /account (A-129) (iteration 10) |
| D-17 | Single-currency listings (VEN10-5 part), full-page contrast node walk in CI (FE10-8 original), per-role reachability crawl (PM10-11), per-unit delivery key pool (VEN10-9) | each withdrawn by its author as costing more than it prevents (iteration 10) |
| D-18 | Notice as a URL code (`?saved=<code>`) (buyer's A-117 alternative) | replayed by Back under no-store and forgeable by a crafted link (iteration 10) |
| D-19 | `image: ${APP_IMAGE}` knob to run the released OCI archive (OPS10-8 original) | a second deployment path for an artifact nobody deploys; withdrawn by infrastructure; the overclaim is fixed by rewording (A-144) (iteration 10) |
| D-20 | Pinning the app's container IP for torrc (staff B-round) | subnet collisions with operators' Docker/VPN ranges, must stay inside bitcoind `rpcallowip`, forces a risky network recreate; `restart: true` (A-134) is verified (iteration 10) |
| D-21 | Trusting `X-Forwarded-Host` or a `PUBLIC_ORIGIN` setting (A-138 alternatives) | one more setting to get wrong; forwarding Host fixes it (iteration 10) |
| D-22 | Public-Tor reachability or a metrics endpoint as required CI/product surface | network flake would make "CI passed" unreliable (nightly A-145 instead); a metrics endpoint contradicts the no-telemetry posture (iteration 10) |
| D-8 | Admin password reset for users; fail-on-skip mode for DB tests | reset is a takeover path with no email; CI already fails on an unreachable DB URL (PM NOT WORTH, DA-9) |
| D-23 | A `UNIQUE (lower(handle))` index (SEC11-3 original) | fails every upgrade where "Alice" and "alice" already coexist (SQLSTATE 23505 → startup crash loop); A-87's non-unique index + refusal under the advisory lock is race-free because only register/setup/CLI create users (backend B-round) |
| D-24 | A-132's per-handle "CAPTCHA only after a failed password" trigger; a separate `register:` limiter key as the whole lockout fix | per-handle: every unknown handle is a free dummy-hash bcrypt with no CAPTCHA (BE11-E1), reopening A-151; register key alone: wrong passwords on `/login` cost the same (staff, security B-round) |
| D-25 | Binding the recovery-code / pending-secret reveal to the issuing session (BE11-4 original) | schema for an attacker who already has the password and a session and can change the password; A-152's password rule closes the realistic path (security B-round) |
| D-26 | Web path to grant or revoke administrator; refusing PGP sign-in when the key expires within 30 days (BUY11-1 optional part) | a hijacked admin session could mint a backdoor admin (staff); expiry display + notification carry the value, the refusal only catches a rare case (buyer B-round) |
| D-27 | Dispute assignee column, "Handle this dispute" and audited take-over (STAFF11C-1 original) | new state that goes stale on suspension/demotion (A-118's lesson) for a benefit that needs ≥3 active staff; A-162's metadata closes "decided blind" with one query (devil's advocate, vendor, backend; staff conceded) — revisit after A-169 |
| D-28 | Per-buyer per-listing reservation cooldown (VEN11-2 part); refusing vendor edits on an admin-archived listing (BE11C-2 part) | cooldown is dodged with a second account and the per-buyer cap already bounds concurrency (backend); edits to a hidden listing publish nothing and are how a vendor fixes the cause (vendor, backend conceded) |
| D-29 | freshclam as the default signature path in either mode (direct, via the onion Tor, or a dedicated Tor client), and a signature volume by default (offline import or UI's VERSION-compare volume) | seed trap (SRE), CDN blocks and UUID UA over Tor (SEC, PRIV, DA), three new services and a Docker-version gate for a path the seats' own evidence predicts fails; image-only + host pull is the egress class upgrades already use (C1); Tor freshclam stays A-195 |
| D-30 | SRE-9 original: sha256 stub rows, `media_recovery_required` restore gate, password+TOTP purge; PRIV's `backups/purges.log`; SEC-8 original hide/restore for images | digest = confirmation oracle (SEC) and permanent fingerprint (PRIV); the gate blanks every image after each restore; the app has no writable host path for a log; hide keeps content staff judged unfit (all seats converged on hard delete + restore line, D4) |
| D-31 | Input formats beyond JPEG+PNG (WebP, HEIC, AVIF, GIF, TIFF, SVG), libvips/ImageMagick/libheif, a separate decode sandbox, "already-conforming JPEG" passthrough | C decoders of attacker bytes for no product need; Go decoders are memory-safe; passthrough keeps EXIF/XMP/ICC (PRIV-2) |
| D-32 | Filesystem/object storage, external/remote/cloud clamd, configurable remote `CLAMD_ADDR`, multi-engine scanning, cgo libclamav or ClamAV inside the app container, `_base` image with first-run download, bundling ClamAV in the release archive, backing up a signature volume, `depends_on`/`/healthz` on clamd, rescanning stored images after updates | new egress or trust boundary, or couples market health to the scanner (SRE, SEC, DA); stored bytes are the encoder's output |
| D-33 | Browser-side preview/resize/crop/drag-drop or metadata stripping, async scanning with a pending state, photo reordering, srcset/multiple sizes beyond thumb+full, `:target` lightboxes, mandatory alt text, `capture` attribute | needs JavaScript or adds state with no no-JS payoff (UI) |
| D-34 | Content-hash dedup or blocklist, storing originals or hashes as evidence, quarantine of refused uploads, signed/expiring/per-viewer image URLs, encrypting image bytes at rest, a separate image origin/onion, CSAM/perceptual hashing (gap disclosed instead), PRNU scrubbing, coarsening `created`, publishing signature version/age on `/canary`, one-year `immutable` caching | each keeps or publishes something content-derived, or adds a key/URL scheme with no attacker it stops (SEC, PRIV, DA); one year keeps taken-down images in disk caches (D7) |
| D-35 | Showing the matched signature name to the uploader or in audit rows; letting a clean verdict skip the re-encode; scanning the re-encoded output as "the scan" (SEC-6 retracted); ClamAV heuristics beyond `AlertExceedsMax` by default | false positives would become permanent signed records (C4); the re-encoder is the boundary and the scan of received bytes is the owner's mandate (D5); heuristics pending A-197 |
| D-36 | Buyer uploads; avatars on cards, messages or orders; a moderator upload surface (unless owner Q1 names one) | no surface displays them (UI-10); every extra viewer of an avatar is a correlation point (PRIV) |
| D-37 | Operator refund-review cap (`REFUND_REVIEW_ABOVE_BTC/XMR`) so refunds up to the price are reviewed (SEC-1 optional) | splitting across free orders defeats it while every legitimate full-price refund lands on staff; honest copy and operator disclosure (A-212) instead; revisit only on an operator request for velocity controls (security conceded; DA, buyer, payments; iteration 18) |
| D-38 | Decode `transfer` amount+fee and flag a wallet that ignores `subtract_fee_from_outputs` (PAY-4, SEC-5) | the pinned v0.18.5.1 wallet was measured honouring it on a real offline stagenet fork; adds a classification branch to the single-send path; A-174 covers unverified wallet versions (iteration 18) |
| D-39 | Automatic second refund for a deposit that arrives after settlement | conflicts with one settlement per order, reopens the DA9-1 dust / Monero fee drain and forwards third-party-chosen coins to a buyer's address; disclosure (A-211) and a recorded hand refund (A-31) instead (all four seats; iteration 18) |
| D-40 | Align the return's large-hold threshold with the refund rule (held when excess > 0 above price) | would hold every return; a return needs a completion and more than twice the price paid before it is held — state the asymmetry instead (A-212) (security; iteration 18) |
| D-41 | Handing users a market-signed PSBT so their wallet can broadcast (BUY-13) | a signature nobody broadcast is invisible to chain reconciliation, so a stale database after restore could sign a second outcome that RBF-replaces the first (PAYE P1); retracted by buyer (WR-escrow) |
| D-42 | Publishing the arbiter xpub in the canary or a vendor's account xpub on their page (SEC-5 original, BUY-15) | anyone could derive every escrow and trace every customer's funding (privacy BLOCKER class); fingerprint pin + server-side checks instead; revised by security (WR-escrow) |
| D-43 | Arbiter key held outside Core and passed to `descriptorprocesspsbt` (PAYE-5 original) | puts the key in app memory and RPC bodies; `keypoolrefill` reaches high indices in a whitelisted Core wallet (probed); withdrawn by payments (WR-escrow) |
| D-44 | New order states for funding/claim/settlement; a separate escrow provider registry or watcher pass (PAYE-11 part) | settlement lives in its own table; a second registry duplicates Check, the network and tip guards and payment_status per currency (ARCH-5) (WR-escrow) |
| D-45 | Refusing pasted PSBTs that carry global xpubs or unknown fields (PRIV-5 original) | honest wallets add them and a refusal strands refunds; safety comes from signing only the stored copy; the paste is discarded instead; withdrawn by privacy (WR-escrow) |
| D-46 | Timelocked one-party or market-alone recovery paths; taproot `sortedmulti_a`; a Go BIP32/PSBT library; converting open pooled orders into escrows on chain | custody by timeout contradicts owner answer 1; wallet support unproven; Core RPCs cover parsing and derivation; a conversion is a pooled send with all its risk (WR-escrow) |
| D-47 | ~~Requiring the buyer's PSBT signature for every physical release (SEC-14 original)~~ — REVERSED by the owner 2026-09-29: the owner chose "buyer must sign the release" (ES-11); kept here only as the record of the council's contrary advice (adds no protection under 2-of-3 and costs every buyer a PSBT round trip) |
| D-48 | App-held sealed market tprv for per-order (shape (b)) signing (ARCH-5) | an app compromise or a stolen database plus env yields a permanent market key; a socket-only signer keeps today's cannot-export property (A-255/A-249 ruling P4-R2; architect retracted) |
| D-49 | A per-escrow chain column; a genesis-hash pin | one chain per database once any escrow exists; genesis cannot separate signets or regtest instances and duplicates the chain name otherwise (P4-R4) |
| D-50 | A project-owned signet miner or Core's fetched contrib/signet/miner | Core's own generatetoaddress with a large maxtries mines a private signet (P4 facts) |
| D-51 | Cutover by existing configuration (pause intake or unconfigure pooled); a separate pooled-only switch | the pause blocks escrow and an unconfigured provider strands open pooled orders; the third intake value reuses the lock, form and audit (P4-R8) |
| D-52 | A new administrator acknowledgement or an operator-attested balance as drain-gate inputs | the existing disposition is the acknowledgement and the horizon counts from issuance; a balance cannot be checked by code (P4-R9) |
| D-53 | Dropping pooled tables in any release | escrow's address-reuse refusals, order history and the audit export read them permanently (P4-R10) |
| D-54 | Pointing the regtest scripts at public nodes; a public-network smoke in CI; -onlynet=onion by default; sharing the onion container's SocksPort; whitelisting abortprivatebroadcast; a new settlement state for "queued" | weakens the scripts' fund guards; flaky external dependency; stalls sync; breaks deliberate isolation; lets a compromised app suppress broadcasts; changes 074's CHECK for a derivable fact (P4-R7, R16) |
| D-55 | A secp256k1 library or crypto/elliptic for public derivation; hardened per-order indexes; one fixed market xpub in every per-order wallet; mixing shapes within one escrow | math/big plus a Core cross-check suffices and crypto/elliptic assumes a = -3; hardened indexes need a per-order paste; a fixed xpub links every escrow; mixed shapes need private key export (P4-R2) |
| D-56 | A stored high-water-mark sync guard for escrow | brings A-147's stall to escrow; the stateless guard covers the cases (P4-R3) |
